Every step is greater than one; the largest step is the central bridge.
All choices depend only on the fixed horizon, before an oracle is queried.

Define signed template moments

    k_2=M_2+6epsilon,
    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j for j>=2.                       (1)

For an H2 block whose terminal gradient is the scalar v, prescribe

    g_0=(2-c+6epsilon/b0)v,
    g_1=v, g_2=c v, g_3=v.                           (2)

Start this block at x_0=k_2 v and use its actual GD steps. Directly,
b0 g_0+b1 g_1+b0 g_2+g_3=k_2 v, so x_3=v.
At a higher H_(j+1) block with terminal gradient v, use the level-j
template with terminal v on the right and with terminal

    v_left=-k_j v/d_j

on the left. Start the parent at x_start=k_(j+1)v. Since its left queried
displacement is (k_j-1)v_left, its left endpoint is v_left. Its bridge
then ends at -d_j v_left=k_j v, exactly the prescribed right start.
The right endpoint is v. This proves the actual chronological GD recursion
of the formal positions and gradients at every level.

Use terminal gradient 1 at the root. Thus

    x_0=k_m, x_n=g_n=1,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.

For each scalar sample put y_i=x_i-g_i. Every H2 seed endpoint has y=0.
Inside a seed with terminal v, define G0=2-c+6epsilon/b0. Its gradients
and proximal coordinates, in order, are

    gradients:   (G0, 1, c, 1) v,
    proximal:    (k_2-G0, 1+d_1+b0 c, 1+a c, 0) v.     (3)

The template is not legal. In the rightmost seed the two queried indices
i=n-2 and j=n-1 have gradients 1,c and displacement x_i-x_j=b1.
Their sum of smooth-convex residuals, independently of function values, is

    Q_ij+Q_ji=(c-1)(d_1+c)>0,                        (4)

where Q is defined below. Thus no scalar function can realize these jets.

For the size estimates needed in the repair, the critical ratio obeys

    R0=k_2/(c d_2)<1-epsilon/4,
    |k_j|=(1-epsilon)c^j R0^(2^(j-2)).                (5)

Here is a direct verification of the strict gap. Since M_2=5-epsilon+2a,

    (c d_2-M_2)/epsilon
      =[6sqrt(2)+epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2
                                  -lambda^2 epsilon^3]/(1+epsilon)>8.

For the last inequality subtract 8 after multiplying by 1+epsilon and use

    6sqrt(2)-8-7epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^3
      >2/5-7/128-12/128^2-6/128^3>0.

It follows that c d_2-k_2>2epsilon, while c d_2<25/4. This proves the
first inequality in (5); the second follows by squaring the ratio at each
step of (1). Every absolute left-child scale |k_j|/d_j is less than c.
Consequently every seed's absolute terminal amplitude is at most c^(m-2).

The elementary bounds c<5/2, k_2<6, |G0|<1, and b0<sqrt(2)<3/2 give
from (3)

    |g_i|<3c^m, |y_i|<7c^m                           (6)

for every sample. For clarity, k_2<6 follows from
M_2<5+6/7 and 6epsilon<=3/64; and
G0 lies between -1/2 and 2-12/5+3/64<0. These bounds include the terminal
sample, with the optimum sample g_star=y_star=0 added separately.

Equation (5) also gives

    |k_m|<c^m exp[-2^(m-4)/m] ->0.

Thus the formal choice f_n=1/2 would have an enormous ratio 1/k_m^2. It
is precisely this initially tiny normalization that the repair must pay for.

## 2. One explicit extra coordinate repairs ALL interpolation inequalities

Choose any curvature

    0<kappa<1/h_max,

and define the monotone quadratic trajectory

    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i,
    xi_star=0.

All xi_i are positive and strictly decreasing. This is a single extra
coordinate; no incompatible instances are averaged. For a scalar T>0
set the proposed two-dimensional jets to

    X_i=(x_i,T xi_i),
    G_i=(g_i,T kappa xi_i),
    f_i=[g_i^2+T^2 kappa xi_i^2]/2,
    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                        (7)

The entire vector GD recursion is exact:
X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i G_i. The scalar first coordinate by itself is still not a
legal function; the final interpolating function will be jointly convex
in both coordinates and is not asserted to be a separable sum.

Use the accepted interpolation convention

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2.

Direct expansion gives the FULL all-pair law, including every star pair:

    Q_ij=g_i(y_j-y_i)
                   -T^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2. (8)

The first term follows by substituting f_i^formal=g_i^2/2 and x_i=g_i+y_i;
the second is the residual for the quadratic t -> kappa t^2/2. Thus the
same function values and the same two coordinates are tested against all
ordered pairs, not merely against one aggregated dual form.

From (6), g_i(y_j-y_i)<=42c^(2m). For any two distinct finite indices,
their xi displacement is at least one adjacent displacement, and

    xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa xi_n.

For an optimum pair xi_i>=xi_n>kappa xi_n, since kappa<1. Therefore
the completely explicit choice

    T^2=84c^(2m)/[kappa^3(1-kappa)xi_n^2]+k_m^2        (9)

makes every Q_ij<=0. There is no optimization or nearest-feasible-point
problem hidden in (9): every quantity is an explicit scalar or product
of the predetermined steps.

To produce one actual function, let J={star,0,...,n}, and define on R^2

    H(v)=max_(i in J) {
       <G_i,X_i>-f_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2 },
    F(X)=sup_v {<X,v>-H(v)}.                         (10)

By accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, F is finite everywhere, convex,
differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant one, and interpolates every
jet in (7). Formally this use of the predecessor is obtained by appending
one zero step to h, so all indices 0,...,n are queried prefix samples.
The predecessor explicitly permits that zero step. All the required
prefix interpolation inequalities were proved in (8)-(9).

The minimizer of F is UNIQUE and equals zero. Indeed write

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+
           max_(i in J){<X_i-G_i,v>+||G_i||^2/2-f_i}.

At v=0, the star term is zero, whereas every other intercept is

    ||G_i||^2/2-f_i=-T^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2<0.

There are finitely many terms. In a neighborhood of zero only the star
term is active, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 there. The set of minimizers of its
conjugate is the subgradient set of H at zero, which here is {0}.
This last assertion can also be checked from the defining supremum:
F(X)=0 requires <X,v><=H(v) for all v, hence X=0 by taking arbitrarily
small positive and negative v; and H(v)>=||v||^2/2 gives F(0)=0.

Consequently the TRUE squared distance to the closest minimizer is

    dist(X_0,argmin F)^2=k_m^2+T^2.                  (11)

There is no artificial enlargement from choosing a remote minimizer.
The exact normalized terminal payoff of the constructed function is

    P_assigned=[1+T^2 kappa xi_n^2]/[k_m^2+T^2].       (12)

Multiplying F by any L>0 gives a convex function with L-Lipschitz gradient,
the same GD trajectory for steps h_i/L, and the same project-normalized
payoff 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2.

## 3. This is a nonzero legal witness, with a fully quantified scale

For the explicit choice kappa=1/(2S), we have kappa h_i<=1/2, so it is in
the permitted range. For 0<=z<=1/2,

    log(1-z)>=-2z,

because the derivative of log(1-z)+2z is (1-2z)/(1-z)>=0 and its value
at zero is zero. It follows that xi_n>=exp(-2kappa S)=exp(-1).
Since (9) gives T^2>=k_m^2, (12) yields the explicit lower bound

    P_assigned >= kappa xi_n^2/2
                       >=exp(-2)/(4S).               (13)

This is an actual legal two-dimensional witness with order 1/S payoff.
It does not claim a new worst-case lower bound beyond what simpler
quadratic or Huber examples already provide. Its purpose is to make the
proposed deformation of the formal template fully concrete and legal.

## 4. One retained QUERY pair forces a cubic normalization cost

The cost is not an artifact of the conservative sufficient amplitude (9).
Take ANY T>0 and ANY function values for which the queried positions and
gradients (7) are jointly legal with minimizer zero. Keep only the queried
pair i=n-2, j=n-1; no terminal sample is used in this argument.

In the first coordinate the rightmost H2 seed has

    g_i=1, g_j=c, x_i-x_j=b1,
    Q_ij^formal+Q_ji^formal=(c-1)(d_1+c)=:D_bad.

Function values cancel in this sum, so these equalities remain valid for
arbitrary proposed values. In the second coordinate

    xi_i-xi_j=kappa b1 xi_i.

The necessary sum of the TWO original interpolation inequalities is
therefore exactly

    D_bad-T^2 kappa^3(1-kappa)b1^2 xi_(n-2)^2<=0.

Thus every feasible repair in this stated geometry must obey

    T^2 >= D_bad/[kappa^3(1-kappa)b1^2 xi_(n-2)^2].    (14)

We have D_bad>4: c-1>4/3 and d_1+c>3. Also b1<=2 and xi_(n-2)<=1.
Consequently

    1/T^2<kappa^3, and T^2>kappa^(-3)>h_max^3.        (15)

This is a necessary inequality for the original queried geometry. Changing
function values or applying maximal completion at the last unqueried point
cannot remove it. It explains the cubic price: the quadratic coordinate's
adjacent displacement is proportional to kappa, and its interpolation
slack is proportional to another kappa times the squared displacement.

For the assigned terminal values, combining (12),(15) gives

    P_assigned < kappa xi_n^2+kappa^3
               <=1/[2 exp(1) S]+h_max^(-3).           (16)

Here xi_n<=exp(-kappa S), from 1-z<=exp(-z), and
max_(t>=0) t exp(-2St)=1/[2 exp(1)S], as follows by differentiating.

## 5. Maximal terminal completion also pays the cost

Keep only the queried jets i<n with the assigned values (7), together
with the optimum. Use the accepted maximal prefix interpolant from
`1252b8823bb5a290` to complete the last unqueried point. This is a legal
single global function, and its value at X_n is maximal among all
interpolants of those prefix jets. The strict negative intercepts in
Section 2 still apply to every retained non-star jet, so its minimizer
is again uniquely zero. Thus (11) is still the actual closest-minimizer
distance. No terminal gradient or terminal value is imposed in this part.

There is a uniform upper bound on this maximal terminal payoff. More
generally it applies to any legal prefix with the displayed X_i,G_i and
minimizer zero, for its ratio normalized by ||X_0||^2. Let i=n-1 be the
last queried point. Interpolation with the optimum gives

    f_i<=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2.

The smoothness inequality on the actual final step X_n=X_i-b0 G_i gives

    F(X_n)<=f_i-b0(1-b0/2)||G_i||^2.

These two inequalities imply

    2F(X_n)<=2<G_i,X_i>-(1+2b0-b0^2)||G_i||^2.        (17)

Here g_i=c, x_i=1+b0 c, and the second coordinate is (T xi_i,T kappa xi_i).
Since 1+2b0-b0^2>0, the right side is at most

    2c+(b0^2-1)c^2+2T^2 kappa xi_i^2
                     <12+2T^2 kappa xi_i^2.

Divide by k_m^2+T^2, apply (15), and use
xi_i<=exp[-kappa(S-b0)]. The normalized completed payoff satisfies

    P_completed < 12kappa^3+2kappa exp[-2kappa(S-b0)]
                 <=12h_max^(-3)+1/[exp(1)(S-b0)].     (18)

The denominator S-b0 is positive. This bound holds uniformly in the
curvature 0<kappa<1/h_max and in every feasible amplitude T. In particular
it covers the explicit repair (9) and exact maximal unqueried-terminal
completion. Every queried-prefix constraint, especially (14), is retained.

As m tends to infinity, S tends to infinity and b0 stays bounded. Also

    M_m<=1+n h_max<2^m h_max,
    h_max>(127/128)lambda^(m-2),
    M_m/h_max^3 < 2^m/h_max^2 ->0.

The last limit follows from 2/lambda^2<1. Equations (16),(18) therefore
give the explicit leading-scale bounds

    limsup M_m P_assigned <=1/[2 exp(1)],
    limsup M_m P_completed <=1/exp(1)<1.              (19)

Hence this whole monotone single-quadratic-coordinate repair mechanism
cannot supply a witness separating the schedule's actual worst case from
the proposed objective level 1/M_m. Its canonical repaired functions have
a genuine unique minimizer, so this failure is not caused by a loose choice
of normalization point. It is caused by the retained adjacent queried
pair (4), which forces the normalization cost (14).

## 6. Exact scope and the remaining repair requirement

The constructive statement covers the original C55 formal trajectory
with one monotone quadratic auxiliary coordinate and the explicit
conjugate function (10). The necessary cost (14) does not depend on the
values, while the unique-minimizer assertion is proved for the two
canonical functions with assigned prefix values: the full-jet interpolant
and its maximal unqueried-terminal completion. For arbitrary different
prefix values or different interpolants, (18) concerns the specified
minimizer zero; it does not silently assert that zero is their closest
minimizer.

No assertion is made about oscillating auxiliary coordinates, multiple
independent repair directions, a changed first-coordinate trajectory,
different knot/value rules, or a completely different adversary. The
result also does not claim that every fixed-dimensional witness has this
cost. The curvature restriction 0<kappa<1/h_max is essential to this
particular monotone repair and is displayed throughout.

The original target quantifies over arbitrary nonnegative schedules,
including zeros and unbounded positive steps. The present construction
uses a strictly positive schedule with growing bridges; it does not
replace those quantifiers. The appended zero step is only a precise way
to apply the already accepted full-prefix interpolation theorem.

For the next primal repair, a concrete requirement is now visible: the
auxiliary coordinates must supply order-one adjacent co-coercivity at
the rightmost H2 defect without paying order kappa^(-3) initial energy,
or the scalar gradients at that pair must be changed. This is a test of
a proposed new construction, not a theorem-equivalent claim that such a
repair exists or has negligible cost.

All new claims here remain draft evidence. There is no new accepted fact,
no unrestricted upper bound for W_n(H_m), and no resolution of the exact
Zhang--Jiang profile problem.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001272 -->
# General removal of terminal credit with an explicit physical contraction

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. This result changes
the physical steps and does not prove same-schedule credit re-selection.
It uses all actual interpolation coefficients and a full PSD identity.
No solver or verifier call, and no external theorem used as a premise.

## 1. Statement, including zero steps

Fix n>=0 and a nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Write
s=sum h_i, C=1+s. On independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_n and values
f_0,...,f_n, substitute x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and put

 Q_ij^h=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                            -s(s+1)||g_n||^2,
 J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n)^h,
 E_theta=V_h-theta J_h.

Suppose 0<=theta<2 and actual nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij for all
distinct finite pairs give an exact certificate

              E_theta+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij^h=M,       (1)

where all value coefficients vanish and M is PSD on the FULL independent
gradient space. This includes the project range 0<=theta<1.

Define positive parameters and the contracted schedule by

 a=1-theta/2,
 kappa=theta C/(2-theta),
 t=1/(1+kappa)=(2-theta)/(2+theta s),
 c=2/(2+theta s),
 h'_i=t h_i,     Lambda'_ij=c Lambda_ij.                  (2)

Then Lambda' is an ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_(h') with the
complete PSD remainder

 V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')
       =(t^2/a)[M+kappa(S_Lambda+a Var_h)],               (3)

where

 S_Lambda=(1/2)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2,
 Var_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2+||g_n||^2
                     -(1/C)||sum_(i<n)h_i g_i+g_n||^2.   (4)

Both added forms are PSD, with no missing gradient directions. Every
actual output multiplier is nonnegative because c>0. Zero steps remain
zero, and the proof works on the original formal space without identifying
duplicate gradients or invoking compression. Empty and all-zero schedules
are included.

The actual physical capacity after this same-length conversion is exactly

             C'=1+sum h'_i=2C/[2+theta(C-1)].             (5)

No prefix or bridge is appended. The loss in (5) must be charged before
claiming an improving ordinary seed.

## 2. Two identities before changing the schedule

Put H_g=sum_(i<n)h_i g_i and define the rank-at-most-two quadratic

                    D_h=||H_g+g_n||^2-C^2||g_n||^2.

Direct expansion gives

                    V_h-2J_h=D_h,                       (6)
                    E_theta=a V_h+(1-a)D_h.

Here is an explicit verification of (6). The value terms cancel. Write
x_n-x_i=-sum_(j=i)^(n-1)h_j g_j in J_h. Expanding 2J_h and canceling the
diagonal norm terms in V_h leaves

 2sum_(i<=j<n)h_i h_j<g_i,g_j>-sum_(i<n)h_i^2||g_i||^2
     +2<H_g,g_n>-[s(s+1)+s]||g_n||^2.

The first two terms equal ||H_g||^2, and s(s+1)+s=C^2-1. The displayed
expression is therefore D_h, proving (6).

Also define the formal scalar value combination

                    F_h=sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n).

The value part of E_theta is 2a F_h. Exact cancellation in (1) forces

              sum Lambda_ij(f_i-f_j)=-2a F_h.            (7)

This checks the complete finite-node flow, not only its total sum.

## 3. Complete coefficient identity under contraction

All query differences scale by t, so, as an identity in independent
values and gradients,

 Q_ij^(h')=t Q_ij^h
                +(1-t)[f_i-f_j+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].         (8)

Let K_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2-s||g_n||^2. The value and gradient terms of
V_(h') give exactly

                 V_(h')=t^2 V_h+t(1-t)(2F_h+K_h).       (9)

Using Lambda'=c Lambda, equations (1), (6), (7), and (8) now yield

 V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')
  =V_(h')+ct[M-aV_h-(1-a)D_h]
                         +c(1-t)[-2aF_h+S_Lambda].      (10)

The parameter identities t=ac, ct=t^2/a, 1-t=kappa t and
1-a=a kappa/C are exact consequences of (2). Substitute (9) into (10):
the V_h coefficients cancel since cta=t^2, and the F_h coefficients cancel
since ca=t. The remaining expression is

 ct M+t(1-t)K_h-ct(1-a)D_h+c(1-t)S_Lambda
   =ct[M+kappa S_Lambda+a kappa(K_h-D_h/C)].              (11)

Finally K_h-D_h/C is precisely Var_h, because C-s=1. Equations (11) and
ct=t^2/a prove the entire formal identity (3).

## 4. Positivity on the full formal space

S_Lambda is a nonnegative sum of squared gradient differences. For Var_h,
assign weights w_i=h_i for i<n and w_n=1, whose sum is C>0. Expanding
the squared differences gives

 Var_h=(1/C)sum_(0<=i<j<=n)w_i w_j||g_i-g_j||^2>=0.     (12)

This remains valid when any h_i vanishes. Since a,t>0 and kappa>=0, (3)
and the assumed full PSD of M prove full PSD of the repaired matrix.
All output interpolation coefficients are positive scalar multiples of
actual nonnegative coefficients, so none of their signs is omitted.
The cancellation of the independent F_h terms in Section 3 explicitly
checks the new value flow. This establishes the statement in Section 1.

For an all-zero schedule, t=a, c=1, Var_h=0, and the input value flow is
zero. Its remainder is M=S_Lambda, so (3) reduces to the same matrix M.
For n=0 every sum of distinct finite pairs is empty and M=0. Thus these
boundary cases satisfy the formula directly.

## 5. Explicit target-relevance cost

Summing the contracted physical steps gives (5). Let K>1 be any desired
capacity threshold for an ordinary seed of this same length. Algebra shows

 C'>K
   iff (2-theta K)C>K(2-theta).                         (13)

Consequently a necessary condition is theta<2/K. When theta<2/K,
condition (13) is equivalent to the explicit input requirement

                    C>K(2-theta)/(2-theta K).            (14)

If theta>=2/K and theta<2, no finite C>=1 can satisfy C'>K: the left side
of (13) is nonpositive and its right side is strictly positive. In
particular, for the previously proposed useful primitive-seed threshold
K=(n+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2), this same-length conversion requires
theta<2/(n+1)^rho and then (14). This is only an exact cost comparison;
the conditional asymptotic amplification argument is not invoked here as
a verified premise, and no seed meeting the threshold has been found.

When theta>0 is fixed and C tends to infinity, C' tends to2/theta. Thus
fixed positive credit cannot yield unbounded ordinary capacity by THIS
conversion. An improving sequence by this mechanism must control theta
at the corresponding inverse-capacity scale. Other conversions with
prefixes or bridges may behave differently and are not excluded.

## 6. A useful corollary about uniform contraction

Given an ordinary nonnegative certificate for h, adding theta h_i to
every incoming-terminal multiplier makes (1) hold with the SAME M for
any 0<=theta<2: those additions cancel -theta J_h. For any prescribed
0<t<=1, choose

                    theta=2(1-t)/(1+t s).

It lies in [0,2), and (2) gives exactly that t. Therefore every uniform
contraction t h of a certified ordinary schedule has the explicit ordinary
certificate (3). This statement concerns certificates on the full formal
space, including zero steps, and does not assert that enlarging steps is
safe or that arbitrary independent contractions preserve the property.

## Remaining issue

This general transformation removes credit by paying an exact physical
sum loss. It neither fixes h nor proves that E_theta and V_h have the same
certificate cone. The same-schedule problem remains open outside the
saturated-cut and weighted-cut repair classes. Any target-level use must
produce a concrete E_theta family surviving (5), an exact ordinary
certificate as above, and the full infinite-horizon benchmark comparison.


Exact audit performed this round: all5 values and15 independent Gram monomials match for the complete h=(1,2,1,1),theta=1/2 certificate, giving h'=h/3 and C'=8/3. Also checked all original independent coefficients with an interior zero-step h=(1,0),theta=3/5 and an all-zero h=(0,0),theta=3/2. All three fixed rational audits passed; no SDP/PEP or verifier call. The tiny exact audit is local_memory/check_credit_contraction.py, results credit_contraction_exact_audit.json.

# Signed factors, zero steps, and the boundary of the two-sequence certificate

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round38,
the second and final analytical round allocated after completed36 by
TASK.md and master_guidance3acdda5439874057. The completed ceiling is38;
attempted ceiling39 includes the historical interruption. There is no
renewal, formal-verifier allowance, or original-target completion claim.

The original Goal remains OPEN. No numerical evaluation, solver,
machine algebra, replay, installation, consult, subordinate agent, or
fact_submit was used. All mathematics below is a textual joint proof.

The new input class permits arbitrary FINITE REAL factors, including
zeros, and EVERY nonnegative physical step. It imposes no alternating,
palindromic, constant, or monotone word pattern. This removes both
positivity restrictions of the earlier two-sequence program. The exact
original entry signs, flow, and zero matrix remainder are retained.

The resulting obstruction is complete for that declared class. Signed
factors do not supply a new actual seed: after a common sign change and
algebraic isolation of zero-step coordinates, every nontrivial member is
a positive-factor free-word certificate. Its capacity is at most the
basic capacity for its number of positive steps, and is strictly less
than C_r at each assigned physical length r=5 or6. Every fixed integer
ray and every actual primitive-leaf substitution have the wrong direction
for the required improvement. Limits of this class still have A<=C_r.

Two further scope results are proved. First, EVERY original nonnegative
zero-remainder certificate, without any factor restriction, excludes
physical steps in (0,1] and has identically zero multiplier rows and
columns at zero steps. Second, a full explicit three-step certificate,
and the accepted basic maximizing words at lengths5 and6, show that
finite signed two-sequence factorization is NOT lossless even inside
the zero-remainder cone. Two factor pairs suffice for the explicit
three-step array. No obstruction to arbitrary sums of factor pairs,
to the entire zero-remainder cone, or to nonzero PSD remainders follows.

Accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, proof Sections2,10--13, for
the original scalar recurrences, the full certificate identity, physical
joins and arbitrary-length substitution; and fact1252b8823bb5a290 for
the interpolation orientation. The unverified drafts0421d0c1066b8f2d
andc1d41aaebcae37de are awareness only. Every new positive-factor
ingredient used here is rederived within this document, including the
hereditary scalar extremal theorem. No unverified result is a premise.

All local auxiliary symbols have implicit suffix sf38. In particular
U_i,D_i below are factors, not the benchmark U_n^ZJ or a dominant mass.

## 1. Original coordinates and the enlarged declared class

Let r>=1, h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) with h_i>=0, s=sum h_i, A=1+s,
and t_i=h_i-1. Retain ALL r+1 independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r
and formal values f_0,...,f_r. At normalized smoothness1 write

 x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji
        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.

For the ORIGINAL array Lambda, require Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j
and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1 and set

 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)).

The required exact flow is

 L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T.                  (1.1)

The original matrix is

 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.                (1.2)

The full primitive cone allows M_prim>=0 on ALL R^(r+1). The class
in this document imposes the additional equality M_prim=0. Accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 supplies the polynomial identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                  =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

Consequently, on writing mu_ij=Lambda_ji/2, zero remainder and flow
are equivalent to

                V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij,  mu>=0.    (1.3)

For the DECLARED TWO-SEQUENCE CLASS choose arbitrary finite real
U_0,...,U_(r-1),D_1,...,D_r. Set U_-1=D_(r+1)=0 and set t_r=0
only as an auxiliary placeholder. For EVERY 0<=i<j<=r define

 mu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),
 mu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),
 Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, Lambda_ji=2mu_ij.                    (1.4)

There is no deleted off-diagonal pair. Membership requires ALL entries
in(1.4) to be nonnegative and requires(1.3), equivalently(1.1) and
M_prim=0. Factors are not assumed positive, nonzero, or monotone.
In particular, a change of internal factor signs is allowed only when
these actual ORIGINAL entry conditions remain true.

## 2. A necessary lemma for the ENTIRE zero-remainder cone

This section assumes only(1.3), not(1.4). Define auxiliary net flow
and the two incoming sums by

 d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji),
 I_i=sum_(j<i)mu_ji, J_i=sum_(j>i)mu_ji.

The function coefficients in(1.3) give d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s.
At a nonterminal i the squared-difference terms have coefficient
-d_i-2I_i-2J_i on ||g_i||^2. A trajectory term can contribute to
this diagonal only from Qbar_(j,i) with j>i; their total contribution
is 2h_i J_i. Matching the diagonal of V_h therefore gives

           t_i J_i-I_i=(1-t_i^2)/2,  0<=i<r.            (2.1)

If 0<h_i<=1, then -1<t_i<=0. The left side of(2.1) is nonpositive,
because I_i,J_i>=0, while its right side is strictly positive. This
is impossible. Thus every positive step is strictly greater than1.

If h_i=0, then t_i=-1 and(2.1) gives I_i+J_i=0. Nonnegativity makes
EVERY incoming mu entry at i zero. Since d_i=h_i=0, every outgoing
entry at i is also zero. The corresponding entire original Lambda row
AND column vanish. This is an algebraic assertion on the full array;
it does not impose equality between any two formal gradients or values.

There is also an exact balance restriction. Substitute the actual scalar
unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1 into(1.3). Its gradients equal its
positions, and every Qbar_ij is zero. Put

 v_0=1, v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i=-t_i v_i.

For these samples the primitive energy is

 V_h=sum_(i<r)h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2-s(s+2)v_r^2
     =sum_(i<r)(v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2)-s(s+2)v_r^2
     =1-A^2 v_r^2.

It must vanish, so

                 A product_(i<r)|t_i|=1.                (2.2)

Here no factorization, irreducibility, or rank assumption has entered.
For positive steps t_i>0; zero steps have |t_i|=1. This necessary
lemma applies to the full ZERO-remainder cone only. It does not extend
to an arbitrary positive semidefinite remainder.

## 3. Finite signed factors collapse to positive factors with zero padding

Assume now(1.4) as well. If s=0, every step is zero; Section2 makes
every multiplier zero, and A=1. Hence suppose s>0.

At the terminal, the total incoming auxiliary weight telescopes to

 I_r=sum_(i<r)mu_ir=U_(r-1)D_r.

The terminal squared-gradient coefficient in sum mu Qbar is s-2I_r.
Matching -sA gives

             U_(r-1)D_r=s(s+2)/2>0.                    (3.1)

Changing both factor sequences to their negatives preserves every
entry in(1.4). Make this change if necessary so that D_r>0. Every
terminal entry then gives

 mu_ir=(U_i-U_(i-1))D_r>=0.

Thus U_-1=0<=U_0<=...<=U_(r-1), with U_(r-1)>0.

Let a be the first index with U_a>0. If i<a, both U_i and U_(i-1)
vanish, so every entry incident to i in(1.4) is zero. Its flow gives
h_i=0. At i=a the entry mu_ar=U_aD_r is strictly positive, so this
row cannot be a zero-step row by Section2. Hence h_a>1 and t_a>0.
For every j>a the actual entry

          mu_ja=t_a U_a(D_j-D_(j+1))>=0

forces D_j>=D_(j+1). Consequently

 D_(a+1)>=D_(a+2)>=...>=D_r>0,
 U_i>=U_a>0 for every i>=a.                             (3.2)

All ACTIVE factors are already positive. Arbitrary D entries preceding
this active suffix cannot affect the array, since all earlier U entries
vanish.

Consider a zero step at i>=a. It has every incident multiplier zero
by Section2. Its terminal edge gives U_i=U_(i-1). Since i>a, the
edge mu_ia=t_aU_a(D_i-D_(i+1)) gives D_i=D_(i+1). A zero step
therefore creates exact plateaus in BOTH active factor sequences.

Let m be the number of positive steps. List their original indices as

 iota(0)<...<iota(m-1),  and set iota(m)=r.

Define the compressed word and factors by

 hhat_j=h_(iota(j)), Uhat_j=U_(iota(j)) for 0<=j<m,
 Dhat_j=D_(iota(j)) for 1<=j<=m,
 Uhat_-1=Dhat_(m+1)=0.

All Uhat and Dhat in their nonboundary ranges are strictly positive.
The U and D plateaus imply that, on every retained ordered pair, the
original formula(1.4) is EXACTLY the same formula for hhat,Uhat,Dhat.
For example U_(iota(j)-1)=Uhat_(j-1), including the first-index zero,
and D_(iota(j)+1)=Dhat_(j+1). The latter identity includes trailing
zero steps and the retained original terminal r.

Every removed row and column was already identically zero. Also its
step is zero, so x_(iota(j)) is the GD position for the compressed word
using its retained formal gradients. Restricting the polynomial identity
to the retained variables proves(1.3) for the compressed word. Conversely
its identity extends to the full original space with zero rows at the
unused independent coordinates. NO quotient relation between gradients
at duplicated positions is used anywhere.

This proves the forward reduction. Its converse is explicit: starting
from any positive-factor word, insert zero steps, copy the neighboring
U and D values across each inserted zero index, put U=0 before the
first active index, and choose the unused leading D entries arbitrarily.
Then every inserted row and column is zero and all retained entries are
unchanged. Thus the enlarged class is exactly the positive-factor
free-word class padded with arbitrary zero steps, apart from irrelevant
factor gauges and unused leading factor values.

## 4. Joint positive-factor capacity proof: coefficient and prefix identities

For this section let the positive compressed length be q>=1, and drop
hats. Thus h_i=1+t_i with t_i>0, U_i,D_j>0 in their nonboundary ranges,
and(1.3)--(1.4) hold with r replaced by q and with U_-1=D_(q+1)=t_q=0.
Nonnegative entries imply

                 U_i>=U_(i-1), D_j>=D_(j+1).             (4.1)

The first implication follows from the terminal entries. The second
follows from mu_(j,0)=t_0U_0(D_j-D_(j+1)). Conversely(4.1) makes
all entries of(1.4) nonnegative when t,U,D are positive.

For i<j, the two telescoping sums are

 sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=U_i(D_j+t_jD_(j+1)),
 sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(j+1).

The coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in sum mu Qbar is

 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)-2h_iU_i(D_j+t_jD_(j+1))
                    +2h_j(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(j+1),

where h_q=1 is an unused auxiliary value. Expanding(1.4) makes this
coefficient zero for every pair; at j=q its last term is zero.
This is a variable-step identity on the entire formal space.

Put p_0=0 and p_k=U_(k-1)D_k for 1<=k<=q. Here

 I_i=U_(i-1)(D_i+t_iD_(i+1)),
 J_i=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(i+1),

with I_0=0. Hence t_iJ_i-I_i=t_i^2p_(i+1)-p_i. Section2 and the
terminal diagonal yield

 t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1,
 2p_q+1=A^2.                                          (4.2)

Define prefix capacity and reciprocal product by

 P_k=1+sum_(i<k)(1+t_i),
 R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1), P_0=R_0=1.

Then

 p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2>0 for 1<=k<=q,
 R_q=P_q=A.                                           (4.3)

In particular every nonempty prefix has R_k>1, even if some individual
excess t_i is greater than1.

Define the normalized weighted prefix and suffix sums

 u_k=(sum_(i<k)h_iU_i)/U_k,
 v_k=(sum_(j=k+2)^q h_(j-1)D_j)/D_(k+1), 0<=k<q.

Outgoing cut weight across {0,...,k} is p_(k+1)(1+v_k):
sum_(j>k)(D_j+t_jD_(j+1))=D_(k+1)+sum_(j>=k+2)h_(j-1)D_j.
Incoming cut weight is p_(k+1)(t_k+u_k). The cut flow is therefore

 p_(k+1)(1-t_k+v_k-u_k)=P_(k+1)-1.                    (4.4)

We prove, for EVERY positive representation,

 u_k=(P_k-R_k)/(1+R_(k+1)),
 v_k=(P_(k+1)-R_(k+1))/(R_(k+1)-1).                    (4.5)

At k=0 the first formula is u_0=0. Inserting t_0=1/R_1 and
p_1=(R_1^2-1)/2 into(4.4) gives the second. For k<q-1 the defining
tail sum is positive, so the formula implies P_(k+1)>R_(k+1).

For induction let 1<=k<q and abbreviate
P=P_k,R=R_k,Y=R_(k+1),z=U_k/U_(k-1)>0. The preceding tail gives
P>R>1. Recurrence and the p products give

 u_k=(u_(k-1)+h_(k-1))/z,
 v_(k-1)=[p_(k+1)/(p_k z)](h_k+v_k).

The induction hypothesis makes

 u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)=(P+R)/(1+R),
 v_(k-1)=(P-R)/(R-1).

By(4.4), v_k=u_k+delta_k, where

 delta_k=2(P+t_k)/(Y^2-1)-1+t_k,
 p_(k+1)(h_k+delta_k)=P+RY.

Elimination gives the quadratic

 (1+R)(P-R)z^2-2(P+RY)z
                  -(Y^2-1)(P+R)/(1+R)=0.               (4.6)

Its roots are

 (P+R)(1+Y)/[(1+R)(P-R)],  (1-Y)/(1+R).

To check them, the discriminant calculation reduces to
(P+RY)^2+(P^2-R^2)(Y^2-1)=(PY+R)^2. The second root is negative,
so the first is forced by z>0. It gives the asserted u formula.
For the v formula use t_kY=R and the identity

 (P-t_kY)(Y-1)+2(P+t_k)+(t_k-1)(Y^2-1)
                      =(Y+1)(P+1+t_k-Y).

This completes the induction, including v_(q-1)=0. Thus

                  1<R_k<P_k,  1<=k<q.                  (4.7)

The forced ratios, useful for auditing every original sign, are

 U_k/U_(k-1)=(P_k+R_k)(1+R_(k+1))
                  /[(1+R_k)(P_k-R_k)],
 D_(k+1)/D_k=(P_k-R_k)(R_(k+1)-1)
                  /[(R_k-1)(P_k+R_k)].                 (4.8)

Together with the positive denominators in(4.7), their exact sign
conditions U_k>=U_(k-1),D_(k+1)<=D_k are equivalently

 t_k>=(P_k-R_k)/(P_k+R_k-2),
 t_k(P_k-R_k-2)<=P_k+R_k.                              (4.9)

The second inequality is deliberately not divided by its possibly
negative coefficient. No equality pattern between different steps has
been assumed. One can independently choose t_0,...,t_(q-2)>0 and set
the last excess to the unique positive root of
t^2+(P_(q-1)+1)t-R_(q-1)=0; this enforces only terminal balance.
Equations(4.7),(4.9) remain necessary sign tests on that free word.

## 5. Every contiguous positive-step interval is product-admissible

For a nonempty interval I=[l,k) in the compressed positive word put

 H_I=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_i, K_I=1+H_I,
 q_I=product_(i=l)^(k-1)t_i.

We prove

                  K_I q_I>=1,                           (5.1)

with strict inequality whenever k<q, and equality for the whole word.
For l=0 this is P_k>=R_k, including(4.7).

For 1<=l<k<=q write P=P_l,R=R_l,H=H_I,z=q_I. Let
b_j=(P_j-R_j)/(R_j-1)=v_(j-1), so b_j>=0 and b_q=0. Splitting
the actual weighted tail and using D monotonicity yields

 b_l=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_iD_(i+1)/D_l+(D_k/D_l)b_k
                                             <=H+b_k.

Here b_l+1=(P-1)/(R-1), b_k+1=z(P+H-1)/(R-z), and both denominators
are positive because R_l,R_k>1. Multiplication and cancellation give

                    z>=1-H(R-1)/(P-1).                  (5.2)

The endpoint inequality R_k<=P_k is R<=z(P+H). Inserting it into
the decreasing right side of(5.2) gives

 z[P-1+H(P+H)]>=P-1+H.

Since P-1+H(P+H)=(H+1)(P-1+H), this proves z>=1/(1+H).
If k<q the endpoint inequality is strict, giving strictness in(5.1).
This is a theorem about the declared factors, not about every original
zero-remainder array.

## 6. Exact scalar extremum of the hereditary relaxation

For any length q>=1 consider positive t_i, h_i=1+t_i satisfying

 (1+sum_(i in I)h_i) product_(i in I)t_i>=1
                         for EVERY nonempty contiguous I,
 A product_(i<q)t_i=1, A=1+sum_(i<q)h_i.                (6.1)

The empty word has capacity1. Define the ORIGINAL benchmark by

 Omega(a,c)=(a+c+sqrt((a+c)^2+4ac))/2,
 C_0=1, C_q=max_(0<=j<q)Omega(C_j,C_(q-j-1)).             (6.2)

The maximum capacity in(6.1) is exactly C_q. Moreover every maximizer
has a recursive maximizing split at one bridge index. The following
argument proves the statement without importing an unverified premise.

The set is compact when nonempty. Its singleton conditions give
t_i>=c_*=sqrt(2)-1>0. Global equality gives
1=A product_i t_i>=t_j c_*^(q-1), so t_j<=c_*^(1-q). The constraints
are closed on this bounded positive box, and A is continuous.

Fix a maximizer. Write F_I=log(1+sum_I h_i)+sum_I log t_i, and set
F_empty=0. Call an interval tight when F_I=0. Two tight intervals
cannot overlap without containment. Indeed, if their left-only, common,
right-only pieces have masses a>0,b>0,c>0, then

 F_I+F_J-F_(I union J)-F_(I intersection J)
  =log[(1+a+b)(1+b+c)/((1+b)(1+a+b+c))]>0,

because the numerator minus denominator in the ratio is ac>0. Tightness
of I,J and nonnegativity of the other two F values contradict this.
Nor can two nonempty tight intervals be adjacent: their union is an
interval, and
F_I+F_J-F_(I union J)=log[(1+a)(1+c)/(1+a+c)]>0.

The inclusion-maximal proper tight intervals are therefore disjoint,
and every two are separated by at least one uncovered index. At least
one index is uncovered: a single proper interval cannot cover the whole
word, and multiple intervals covering it would include an adjacent pair.

There cannot be two uncovered indices u,v. Perturb t_u to t_u+epsilon
and t_v to t_v-epsilon, with a sufficiently small nonzero epsilon of
sign such that epsilon(t_v-t_u)<=0. Positivity is preserved. The total
capacity stays fixed, every proper tight interval is unchanged, and all
other proper inequalities stay strict by continuity and finiteness.
But the changed two-factor product is

 t_u t_v+epsilon(t_v-t_u)-epsilon^2<t_u t_v.

The global expression A product t_i is now less than1. Increase t_u
alone until this expression returns to1. Such an increase exists because
the expression is continuous, strictly increasing in t_u and unbounded.
All proper interval expressions are nondecreasing under this increase,
so none becomes invalid. The resulting feasible word has strictly larger
capacity, a contradiction.

There is exactly one uncovered index j. All indices to its left form
one proper tight interval, if any; otherwise two maximal intervals on
that side would require another uncovered index between them. The same
holds on the right. Thus the left and right children are saturated
admissible words (possibly empty). If their capacities are a,c and the
bridge excess is d=t_j, global equality says

                         A=a+c+d, Ad=ac.

Hence A=Omega(a,c). By induction and strict monotonicity of Omega,
A<=Omega(C_j,C_(q-j-1))<=C_q. Equality forces both children to be
maximizers as well. This proves the asserted upper bound and structure.

For attainment, join saturated children of capacities a,c with the
positive d solving d(a+c+d)=ac. Intervals inside a child are admissible.
An interval crossing the bridge has a left suffix of capacity x<=a and
a right prefix of capacity y<=c, where an empty portion has capacity1.
Its product is at least d/(xy) and its capacity is x+y+d. Since

 xy/(x+y+d)<=ac/(a+c+d)=d,

it is admissible. Monotonicity in x,y follows from the positive derivatives
y(y+d)/(x+y+d)^2 and x(x+d)/(x+y+d)^2. The whole joined word has
product d/(ac)=1/(a+c+d). Starting with empty children and a maximizing
split in(6.2) constructs capacity C_q. These are the accepted actual
primitive joins as well as feasible points of the scalar relaxation.

## 7. Strictness at the assigned lengths

Sections5--6 give A<=C_q for every positive-factor compressed length q.
If A=C_q, the maximizing structure in Section6 supplies its unique
uncovered index j. A nonempty left child would be a tight proper prefix,
contrary to the strictness in Section5. Thus j=0 and

                         A<=Omega(1,C_(q-1)).            (7.1)

We show this is strictly smaller than C_q at q=5,6 by exact comparisons.
Let lambda=1+sqrt(2), x=C_2. The balanced scalar identities in accepted
fact04219554e41d9750, Section2, give

 C_3=lambda^2, C_4=Omega(lambda,x),
 C_5=lambda x, C_6=Omega(x,lambda^2),
 x^2-(1+lambda)x-lambda=0.

We have 12/5<lambda<17/7 and 4<x<81/20. The lambda comparisons use
(7/5)^2<2<(10/7)^2. The polynomial for x at4 is12-5lambda<0;
at81/20 with lambda replaced by17/7 it is247/2800>0. Since this
polynomial decreases with lambda, these isolate its positive root.

At X=31/4 the polynomial defining Omega(17/7,81/20) has value1/56>0,
so C_4<31/4. Also C_5>48/5. Put g(X)=X(X-1)/(X+1); its derivative
is positive for X>sqrt(2)-1. Direct comparisons give

 g(48/5)=2064/265>31/4,
 4*2064-31*265=41>0.

As Omega(1,c)<X is equivalent to c<g(X) for the positive quantities
here, Omega(1,C_4)<C_5.

For q=6, x>4 and lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)>29/5. At X=47/4 the polynomial for
Omega(4,29/5) is -23/80<0, so C_6>47/4. Meanwhile C_5<1377/140,
and

 g(47/4)=2021/204>1377/140,
 2021*140-1377*204=2032>0.

Thus Omega(1,C_5)<C_6. Equation(7.1) is impossible at either assigned
length, and every positive-factor member has A<C_5 or A<C_6.

Return to the original signed-factor word of physical length r=5 or6.
If all steps are zero, A=1<C_r. Otherwise let m be its number of
positive steps. Section3 gives A<=C_m. If m=r, the preceding argument
gives A<C_r. If m<r, the original scalar recurrence is strictly increasing:
C_(k+1)>=Omega(1,C_k)>C_k. Again A<=C_m<C_r. Consequently

       EVERY finite real two-sequence member at r=5,6 has A<C_r. (7.2)

At an arbitrary physical length r the same reasoning gives A<=C_m<=C_r.
In particular any coordinatewise limit of words in the declared class,
even with unbounded or differently gauged factors, has A<=C_r by continuity
of the finite sum. Strictness need not persist in that closure. If arrays
also converge, their entry signs, flow and full zero matrix identity
persist by continuity; no bounded-factor representation of the limiting
array has been asserted.

## 8. True lower ray comparisons and the actual physical deficit

For completeness define the original dominant recurrence

 G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
 B_0=1, B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Use W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over the
entire original smooth convex class, all finite dimensions and x_0!=x_star,
with updates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R is unchanged.
Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 proves the positive finite ray limits

 Z_b=lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), b>=1 integer,

and the same limits for every fixed nonnegative terminal offset.

The comparison needed for exclusion is the TRUE LOWER comparison

                   Z_((r+1)b)>=C_r Z_b, every b>=1.     (8.1)

To prove it, take a fully expanded maximizing B_n tree. Replace each
primitive empty leaf by a maximizing primitive tree of length r and
capacity C_r, and its one dominant empty leaf by a maximizing dominant
tree of length r and mass B_r. The accepted elementary comparison
B_r>=C_r, together with homogeneity and monotonicity of Omega and G,
gives an original tree with (r+1)(n+1)-1 steps and mass at least C_r B_n.
The original all-split recurrence therefore gives

                  B_((r+1)(n+1)-1)>=C_r B_n.

Putting n=b*2^j-1 and taking the accepted limits proves(8.1). It is
not a comparison to a sufficient upper enclosure.

For a fixed actual signed-factor seed of physical length r=5 or6 put
C=C_r and delta=C/A-1>0. Section7 and(8.1) show, for EVERY integer b>=1,

                   Z_((r+1)b)/(A Z_b)>=1+delta>1.        (8.2)

The initially requested rays remain b=1, with ratios Z_6/Z_1 and Z_7/Z_1.
No switch of ray can repair this seed class.

The actual substitution keeps the original physical zero steps, if any.
Let H_A be obtained by replacing only the n primitive empty leaves of a
fixed fully expanded maximizing B_n tree by this seed and recomputing
the accepted bridges. Its ONE dominant empty leaf stays physically empty.
Write D_A=1+2sum H_A. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Sections12--13,
gives

 length(H_A)=(r+1)n, W_((r+1)n)(H_A)=1/D_A,
 [sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)]^2<=D_A<=A B_n.                (8.3)

This equality is for the unrestricted all-function W: the accepted
full certificate supplies the upper bound and its actual one-dimensional
Huber trajectory attains it. Zero steps do not alter that argument and
do not remove their physical time indices.

Perform the same substitution using a basic primitive word of the SAME
physical length r and capacity C. This yields an original basic dominant
tree of length (r+1)n, with mass D_C, so B_((r+1)n)>=D_C. Strict
monotonicity of the joins and C>A imply D_C>D_A whenever n>=1. Hence

           W_((r+1)n)(H_A)>U_((r+1)n)^ZJ, every n>=1.   (8.4)

For a quantitative fixed-seed deficit the untouched dominant leaf is paid
using the actual lower bound in(8.3) applied at capacity C:

 B_((r+1)n)W_((r+1)n)(H_A)
 >=[sqrt(C B_n)-(sqrt(C)-1)]^2/(A B_n)
 >=(C/A)(1-B_n^(-1/2))^2.                              (8.5)

Set epsilon=1-sqrt((1+delta/2)/(1+delta))>0. Since the accepted benchmark
has B_n>=2n+1, every n>=1 with 2n+1>=epsilon^(-2) satisfies

             W_((r+1)n)(H_A)>=(1+delta/2)U_((r+1)n)^ZJ. (8.6)

The deficit is seed-dependent. No positive uniform gap across all finite
factorized seeds or their closure is claimed. For a limiting actual
certificate from the closure, A<=C_r gives non-strict physical domination
and a limiting ratio at least1, which already excludes strict improvement.

On an input ray n=b*2^j-1, the ACTUAL output horizon is

                 (r+1)(b*2^j-1).

For b=1 these are6*2^j-6 and7*2^j-7. The accepted substitution gives
the exact limit Z_((r+1)b)/(A Z_b). Neither the four-step193/25 threshold
nor its j25 specialization has been transferred to the present lengths.

## 9. Exact counterexample to losslessness of finite signed factorization

This section gives a scope check, not an improving seed. Set

 w=sqrt(2), lambda=1+w, kappa=lambda^2, h=(w,2,w).

Its physical length is3 and its capacity is C_3=kappa. In the original
ordered indexing 0,1,2,3 define the COMPLETE array

 Lambda =
 [ 0       2                0                 0                 ]
 [ 2lambda 0                0                 kappa-1           ]
 [ 0       (kappa-1)w        0                 2kappa+(kappa-1)w  ]
 [ 0       kappa-1           2kappa*lambda      0                 ]. (9.1)

All entries are nonnegative and every diagonal entry is zero. The
original row sums minus column sums are

                    (-2w,-4,-2w,4lambda),              (9.2)

as required because s=2lambda. To check the entries without decimal
evaluation use kappa=2lambda+1 and lambda*w=lambda+1.
At index0 the difference is2-2lambda=-2w. At index1 it is
4lambda-(4lambda+4)=-4. At index2 it is
(8lambda+6)-(10lambda+4)=-2w. The last difference is4lambda.

The complete zero-remainder identity follows from the accepted primitive
join, and can be specified entirely in the original residuals. A single
step w has

                  V_(w)=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10.

This identity follows by expanding the two displayed residuals, using
(w-1)lambda=1. Join two such children with bridge2. Their capacities
are lambda, and d=1 solves d(2lambda+d)=lambda^2. The accepted exact
join identity is

 V_h=V_left+kappa V_right-(kappa-1)
                    [wQ_21+Q_31+Q_13+wQ_23].           (9.3)

Insert V_left=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10 and
V_right=-2Q_23-2lambda Q_32. The coefficient of every original ordered
Q is exactly the negative of(9.1); all omitted coefficients are zero.
Thus V_h+sum Lambda Q=0 as an identity in ALL four formal gradients
and values. In particular M_prim=0 on all R^4, not merely on actual
trajectories or a selected subspace.

The array(9.1) cannot have ANY finite real two-sequence representation.
If it did, Section3, since every physical step is positive, would give
strictly positive U_0 and D_3 after one common sign change. But
mu_03=U_0D_3>0 would imply Lambda_30>0, whereas(9.1) has Lambda_30=0.

In fact the same WORD has no such representation with ANY other choice
of the original multipliers. Its first proper prefix has

        (1+h_0)(h_0-1)=(1+w)(w-1)=1.

Section5 forces every proper positive-step prefix to have product strictly
greater than1. This is a contradiction independent of the chosen array.

Here is an explicit two-pair representation of the full array, showing
where a larger factor class begins. Use the SAME t=(w-1,1,w-1) for two
copies of formula(1.4), with

 U^(1)=(1,0,0),          D^(1)=(lambda,0,0),
 U^(2)=(0,1,kappa+1),    D^(2)=(0,lambda,lambda).

Set mu=mu^(1)+mu^(2). The only nonzero entries of the first component
are mu^(1)_01=lambda and mu^(1)_10=1. The only nonzero entries of the
second are

 mu^(2)_12=lambda*w, mu^(2)_13=lambda, mu^(2)_31=lambda,
 mu^(2)_23=kappa*lambda, mu^(2)_32=lambda+kappa+1.

All are positive. Substitution into(1.4) gives precisely these entries:
for example (U^(2)_2-U^(2)_1)D^(2)_3=kappa*lambda and
(U^(2)_1+(w-1)U^(2)_2)D^(2)_3=lambda+kappa+1.
Since lambda*w=lambda+1, Lambda=2mu^T is exactly(9.1).
Neither component separately satisfies the full h flow and diagonal
matching. Therefore the obstruction for individual factorized certificates
does not apply to their sum. This is not a mixture of two feasible
full-word certificates.

There are also counterexamples to losslessness at BOTH assigned lengths.
Take an original maximizing primitive tree of length5 or6. Its leaves
have zero remainder; the accepted primitive join preserves zero remainder
and gives a full nonnegative original array with capacity C_5 or C_6.
Every step is positive. Equation(7.2) prohibits any finite signed
two-sequence representation of a certificate for such a word. These
basic words are not strict-threshold seeds; they witness the genuine
restriction of the factorization inside the original zero-remainder cone.

No statement here excludes a limiting factorization with divergent factors
or claims that two pairs span the whole zero-remainder cone. Section7
already controls the capacity of limits of feasible one-pair words.
The hereditary inequality of Section5 remains UNPROVED for arbitrary
full zero-remainder arrays; the example here satisfies it and therefore
does not refute that separate possible theorem.

## 10. Deliverable, remaining interface, and literature scope

The complete obstruction covers all finite real factors and all nonnegative
words in the specific two-sequence construction, as well as the non-strict
capacity bound on its closure. It includes all30/42 original multipliers
and all6/7 independent gradient coordinates at the assigned lengths.
No altered ray, zero padding, or internal sign choice repairs this class.

The full primitive cone is OPEN. Even the full zero-remainder cone has
not been excluded. A new seed needs a genuinely different array, for
example a sum of factor pairs whose individual flows are not prescribed,
or the nonzero-remainder route owned by max4. The concrete missing
implication for a possible larger obstruction is whether EVERY original
nonnegative zero-remainder array forces

 (1+sum_(i in I)h_i)product_(i in I)(h_i-1)>=1

for every contiguous interval in its positive-step compression. Section2
proves only the step/zero-row constraints and global product balance;
Section5 uses one-pair factorization in its weighted-tail comparison.
No claim that the displayed implication holds, and no uncomputed generic
optimization posed as a solution, is part of this handoff.

Source inspiration remains Salah R. Chikhi, *Last-Iterate Performance of
Gradient Descent and Relaxed Proximal Point via s-Composability*,
arXiv:2609.13765v2, Section4, formulas(10)--(14),
https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13765v2. Its precise local text
downloads/2609.13765v2.txt, Definition1 and Section4 including the proof,
was reread. The source proves the constant-step construction. No theorem
there about variable-step signs, signed factors, hereditary capacity,
or the enlarged class is imported. Both Matlas searches returned unrelated
theorems; web search likewise supplied no additional usable theorem.
The new direction reduces by explicit original-coordinate identities to
the positive-factor case, whose complete proof was rechecked and included.

All results in this document remain UNVERIFIED. The assignment explicitly
forbids fact_submit, so no new accepted fact or verifier verdict is claimed.
This completes the final allocated analytical round only. It is not an
inspected worker-process exit, an allocation renewal, a finalized answer,
or a solution of the original unrestricted exact-profile problem.

Local glossary: r is original physical seed length; h is its nonnegative
word; t_i=h_i-1; s=sum h and A=1+s; U,D are finite real factor sequences;
mu is the auxiliary array with Lambda=2mu^T; d_i is its outgoing-minus-
incoming flow; I_i,J_i are incoming weights from smaller/larger indices;
v_i in Section2 is the unit-quadratic gradient; a in Section3 is the first
positive U index; m is the number of positive steps; iota maps compressed
indices to their original active indices and terminal; hats denote that
algebraic compression. In Sections4--7 q is a positive compressed length,
p_k=U_(k-1)D_k, P_k is prefix capacity, R_k is reciprocal prefix product,
u_k,v_k are the explicitly normalized factor sums, z is a local factor
ratio or interval product as defined, and delta_k is the cut-flow offset.
H_I,K_I,q_I denote interval mass, capacity and product; F_I is their
logarithmic defect; c_*=sqrt(2)-1. Omega,C_n,G,B_n,Z_b,lambda,W_n and
U_n^ZJ have the accepted original benchmark meanings. H_A,D_A,D_C are
the actual substituted word and its two mass comparisons; C=C_r,
delta=C/A-1,epsilon is the explicit fixed-seed deficit threshold.
In Section9 w=sqrt(2),kappa=lambda^2 and the superscripts(1),(2) index
the two explicitly specified factor pairs.
