# Max49: three actual faces and a remote-forced return

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission
is authorized. This is the final of the two sessions allocated after
completed47, with completed ceiling49 and attempted ceiling50. The
original all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile goal remains OPEN.

## 1. Result and scope

The construction below changes gradients at the actual queries inside
the original H2 blocks. In one fixed planar triangle its exact sequence
of projection locations is

    original indices 0,1,2,3: the vertical edge,
    index4: upper sloping edge,
    index5: lower sloping edge,
    indices6,7: quadratic core,
    index8: vertical edge again,
    every index from9 onward: quadratic core.

The lower-edge transition at5 happens INSIDE the second physical H2.
The return at8 is forced by a remote original interpolation row. In
particular, observing core entry at6 does not justify a quadratic tail.
Every gradient and value is given by finite formulas; the proof checks
the actual projections against the whole fixed triangle and the optimum.

The result holds uniformly for every integer m>=1000000 and every real
aspect t>=1000. These cutoffs are conservative symbolic enclosures, not
the outcome of a fit or a computation. The aspect may depend arbitrarily
on the entire word and on m. Every child uses the SAME enclosing-m
parameters. There is one static function for each (m,t).

This explicit family is legal but too weak. Its true-distance payoff
V49 obeys

    0<V49<2 Qunit,
    V49<Huber/10<=K_n/10<=F_ray_n/10,
    V49<(sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z))/10,
    0<B_n V49<10(25/48)^m ->0.

All comparisons use the same original physical word. D_h is the FULL
schedule-dependent complex domain, not a lens or a short-cycle screen.
The benchmark B_n is the ORIGINAL all-split recurrence. The bounds
concern this family, not the unrestricted W_n(h).

Two precise forbidden continuations are also proved below. Continuing
the lower affine face at6 fails original row Q_(0,6), even if its new
value is free but all retained values remain fixed. Continuing the
quadratic core through8 fails Q_(8,0); the optimum rows force its value,
so a value repair cannot save that prescribed gradient.

## 2. Correctness inputs and literature use

The accepted inputs, in their stated scopes, are:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: every original directed smooth-convex interpolation
  row and both optimum directions are necessary. Its Q convention is
  used throughout.
* b98bb807d514e80d: full K_n and the original all-split estimate
  B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).
* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: full alternating-ray maximum F_ray_n, including
  every feasible checkpoint set, with K_n<=F_ray_n.
* dfde2fa326c908bf: full D_h, maximal interpolating Gap_h, and the
  legal real parameters used in the complete complex comparison.

Accepted e9a55f57461f253c is only an actual H6 result with epsilon1/6,
dimension66 and a unique minimizer. No new-m transfer or radius claim
is inferred from its dimension. The previously saved H5/H6 observations
motivate changing within-block gradients only. None is recomputed here.

Unverified max48, finding ec0b4a7f59979259, identifies a useful free-aspect
interface, but is NOT a correctness input. Every needed formula and
argument is reproduced here. The older constant-H2 T1/T2 laws are not
retuned or used as premises.

Matlas returned Lemma S17 of arXiv:2107.07257 and Theorem4/Lemma3 of Jai
Moondra, Hassan Mortagy and Swati Gupta, "Reusing Combinatorial Structure:
Faster Iterative Projections over Submodular Base Polytopes,"
arXiv:2106.11943v3 (2023). The exact PDF already exists as
downloads/2106.11943.pdf. The general-polytope definitions, Section3,
Lemma1, Theorem4 and its proof were read in the
[arXiv HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/2106.11943v3). The useful
distinction is between membership in an affine face and the sign of its
normal residual. Here these conditions are proved directly. No external
theorem is a proof premise, and no submodular-polytope assumption is
imposed on the triangle.

## 3. Original word and explicit scalar definitions

Fix m>=1000000, t>=1000 and define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m, beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a, b=beta c,
    A=4+2a+beta, eta=a^2 beta, k=A/(b eta)=A/(a beta^3),
    s=(b+1)/(k-1), D=b+s,
    rho=(a k+1)/(k-1), e=b rho-s,
    N=(A-1)s, C=(A-ell)s, p=ab=beta^2,
    delta=t^(-2), omega=epsilon+delta,
    u=N/(1+delta).
                                                               (1)

All symbols in this draft are local aliases; none renames a project
benchmark or reference. In shared records the new objects carry the
suffix49. In particular C in(1) is a scalar, while the convex triangle
below is denoted T49. The letter e in(1) is a positive edge intercept,
not the exponential constant, and t is an aspect, not a time index.

Define the exact further coefficients, in the following order:

    x5=p-ell u,       v5=C-ell u,
    d5=-(rho x5+v5+e),
    E5=d5/(rho^2+delta),
    j5=x5+rho E5,    w5=v5+delta E5,
    x6=x5-(1+beta)j5, v6=v5-(1+beta)w5,
    gamma=beta^2 c,
    x8=gamma x6,     v8=gamma v6,
    x9=x8-ell,       v9=-a v8.
                                                               (2)

These are finite explicit rational expressions in lambda, m and t.
Their denominators are positive by the bounds in Section4. There is
no unknown Gram matrix, optimization oracle, implicit trajectory solve,
or root-selection problem in these definitions.

Use the three finite vectors

    U=(1,t(D+1)), V=(1,-a t(D+1)), Lvert=(-b,s t),
    T49=conv{U,V,Lvert},
    F49(X)=max_(g in T49) {<X,g>-||g||^2/2},
    X0=(A,t(D+1)).
                                                               (3)

Here Lvert is a vertex label, not the smoothness constant. All vector
inner products and distances are Euclidean. The formula specifies one
static function; its actual gradients are proved below rather than
assumed from the envelope definition.

## 4. Uniform finite-parameter enclosures

The auxiliary coefficients with final subscript0 are algebraic reference
values at epsilon=delta=0. They are NOT substituted for the actual word.
Put

    a0=lambda-2=1/lambda, ell0=lambda-1,
    A0=lambda^2, k0=lambda^3,
    s0=1/(2lambda-1), D0=lambda+s0, rho0=2s0, e0=1,
    z0=lambda s0, T0=lambda^2 s0=1+2s0,
    N0=1+s0, C0=(A0-ell0)s0,
    x50=z0-ell0, v50=-z0,
    d50=(3-lambda)s0^2, E50=(3-lambda)/4,
    j50=-ell0/2, w50=-z0,
    x60=v60=z0, x80=v80=T0,
    x90=a0 s0=1/(3lambda+2), v90=-z0.
                                                               (4)

These identities follow from lambda^2=2lambda+1. For example
(lambda^2-2)(lambda+1)=lambda^3-1, which gives s0; then
(2lambda-1)s0=1 gives rho0=2s0, e0=1, N0=1+s0 and T0=1+2s0.
Also (lambda-1)(2lambda-1)=lambda+3. This last equality gives
x50=z0-ell0, d50=(3-lambda)s0^2, j50=-ell0/2 and x90=a0 s0
by substituting successively into(1)--(2).

Squaring rational endpoints gives

    12/5<lambda<29/12<5/2,
    6/23<s0<5/19,
    29/46<z0<12/19,
    35/23<T0<29/19,
    4/37<x90<5/46,
    1/26<d50<1/24.
                                                               (5)

For z0, the function q/(2q-1) decreases for q>1/2, giving the
displayed endpoints. For d50, the respective product bounds are
21/529 and15/361; they lie strictly between1/26 and1/24.

Here epsilon,delta<=10^(-6) and omega<=2*10^(-6). The basic differences
are bounded as follows:

    |a-a0|<epsilon, |b-lambda|<epsilon, |A-A0|<3epsilon,
    |k-k0|<120epsilon, |s-s0|<4epsilon,
    |D-D0|<5epsilon, |rho-rho0|<3epsilon, |e-1|<13epsilon,
    |N-N0|<22epsilon, |C-C0|<20epsilon.
                                                               (6)

Here are details ensuring that(6) is a finite, uniform estimate.
The exact identities a=a0(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and
b=lambda(1-epsilon^2) prove its first bounds. Since
|beta^3-1|<=3epsilon, one has

    |a beta^3-a0|<(5/2)epsilon,  a beta^3>3/8.

The quotient difference for k is at most
(3+15*(5/2))epsilon/(3/8)=108epsilon<120epsilon, because k0<15.
It also gives13<k<15. Thus k-1>12 and k0-1>12. The quotient for s
is bounded by [1+(1/3)120]epsilon/12<4epsilon.
Using rho=a+(a+1)/(k-1), the difference for rho is bounded by

    epsilon+epsilon/12+(3/2)*120epsilon/144<3epsilon.

In particular s<1/3 and rho<3/5. The difference for e=b rho-s
is below [1+(5/2)*3+4]epsilon<13epsilon. For N, use A0-1<5:
its difference is below 3epsilon/3+5*4epsilon=21epsilon.
For C, use A0-ell0=lambda+2<9/2:
its difference is below4epsilon/3+(9/2)*4epsilon<20epsilon.
These arguments also give the useful strict bounds

    2/5<a<1/2, 12/5<c<5/2, 9/10<beta<1,
    1/4<s<1/3, 1/2<rho<3/5, 9/10<e<11/10,
    D>5/2, 18/5<D+1<15/4, N<4/3,
    0<A<6, b>2, A-1>4.
                                                               (7)

For example D0>12/5+6/23>5/2, and the error5epsilon preserves
this bound. The sharper lower bound D0+1>12/5+6/23+1>18/5
proves the stated lower bound on D+1. All margins in(7) exceed
the corresponding error in(6).

Since u=N/(1+delta),

    |u-N0|<22epsilon+(4/3)delta,
    |ell u-ell0 N0|<35epsilon+2delta.

It follows from |p-1|<2epsilon and(6) that

    |x5-x50|<40omega, |v5-v50|<60omega.
                                                               (8)

In particular |x5|<1. In the d5 expression in(2), use rho0<3/5
to get

    |d5-d50|<3epsilon+(3/5)40omega+60omega+13epsilon
             <=100omega.
                                                               (9)

Consequently

    1/27<d5<1/23.
                                                               (10)

Indeed the larger error allowed in(9) is1/5000, and
1/26-1/5000>1/27 while1/24+1/5000<1/23.
Also rho^2+delta>1/4 and
|rho^2+delta-rho0^2|<4epsilon+delta. The quotient difference gives

    |E5-E50|<4[100omega+(3/20)(4epsilon+delta)]<410omega.

Since E50<3/20, this implies0<E5<1/6. Substitution into(2) now yields

    |j5-j50|<300omega, |w5-w50|<61omega,
    |x6-z0|<650omega, |v6-z0|<190omega.
                                                               (11)

For the first pair, the respective bounds before rounding are
40omega+(1/6)3epsilon+(3/5)410omega and60omega+delta/6.
For x6 and v6 use1+beta=2-epsilon, |j5|<3/4 and |w5|<2/3;
the bounds are40omega+2*300omega+(3/4)epsilon and
60omega+2*61omega+(2/3)epsilon. They prove(11).

Finally |gamma-lambda|<5epsilon, since
beta^2(1+epsilon)=1-epsilon-epsilon^2+epsilon^3. With(11),

    |x8-T0|<1700omega, |v8-T0|<500omega,
    |x9-x90|<1701omega, |v9+z0|<252omega.
                                                               (12)

For x8 the bound is(2/3)5epsilon+(5/2)650omega;
for v8 replace650 by190. For v9 use v8<8/5 and a0<1/2.
Equations(5),(8)--(12) prove the following margins, which will be
used to select the actual faces:

    -3/4<j5<-2/3, -2/3<w5<-3/5,
    3/5<x6<2/3, 3/5<v6<2/3,
    3/2<x8<8/5, 3/2<v8<8/5,
    1/10<x9<3/25, -2/3<v9<-3/5.
                                                               (13)

For the narrowest x9 margin, its error is at most1701/500000,
and4/37-1701/500000>1/10, while5/46+1701/500000<3/25.
For x8, 35/23-1700/500000>3/2 and
29/19+1700/500000<8/5. The other intervals follow using the
smaller errors in(11)--(12) and29/46<z0<12/19.
Thus(13) does not rely on a qualitative continuity assertion.

## 5. The fixed function and the actual first block

The identities D+1=ks and e+rho=a(D+1) show that T49 is exactly
the intersection of the three halfspaces

    X<=1,
    -t X+Y<=t D,
    -rho t X-Y<=t e.
                                                               (14)

The upper line contains U,Lvert, the lower contains V,Lvert,
and the vertical line contains U,V. The other vertex in each case
is strictly on the interior side. All three inequalities are strict
at0 because D,e>0. Thus T49 has nonempty interior containing0.

For any X the maximum in(3) exists and is unique, by compactness of
T49 and strict concavity of the objective in g. Completing the square
shows that its maximizer is the Euclidean projection of X. A vector
g in T49 is that projection if and only if

    <X-g,v-g><=0 for EVERY v in T49.
                                                               (15)

Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance from X along
each segment from g to v; sufficiency follows by expanding the squared
distance. Adding(15) at two inputs proves
||g(X)-g(Z)||^2<=<g(X)-g(Z),X-Z>, hence g is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing-objective inequalities give

    0<=F49(X+d)-F49(X)-<g(X),d><=||d||^2.

This proves differentiability with gradient g, as well as convexity
and global1-smoothness. Because0 is an interior point of T49, the
same formula in(3) is strictly positive at every nonzero X: take a
sufficiently small positive multiple of X as its feasible g. Therefore

    min F49=0, argmin F49={0},
    R49^2:=dist(X0,argmin F49)^2=A^2+t^2(D+1)^2.
                                                               (16)

For the first actual H2, put

    (P0,P1,P2,P3)=(0,ell,ell+1+beta,A-1),
    (T0',T1',T2',T3')=(1,-a,a beta,-eta).

The prime distinguishes these four tangent coefficients from T0 in(4).
For0<=i<=3 define

    Xi=(A-Pi, t(D+1)Ti'),
    Gi=(1,t(D+1)Ti'),
    Yi=Xi-Gi=(A-1-Pi,0),
    fi=||Gi||^2/2+(A-1-Pi).
                                                               (17)

Each Ti' lies in[-a,1], so Gi lies on UV. Each Yi is a nonnegative
multiple of the outward normal of the first inequality in(14).
Thus(15) holds against EVERY vertex and Gi is the actual gradient.
The tangent relation Ti+1'=(1-h_i)Ti' and the partial sums Pi prove
each of the three original updates separately. Because a,beta>0 and
eta<a, all these gradients are distinct at consecutive queries.

At index3 the residual is zero. Using D+1=ks and b eta k=A gives

    X3=G3=(1,-A s t/b),
    X4=-b X3=(-b,A s t).
                                                               (18)

## 6. Upper edge4, lower edge5, and the return at8

The upper normal is n_plus=(-t,1). The difference between its
inner product at X4 and its supporting value tD is Nt. Subtracting
the corresponding normal component gives exactly

    G4=(-b+u,t(s+u)),
    Y4=X4-G4=(Nt/(1+t^2)) n_plus,
    f4=<X4,G4>-||G4||^2/2.
                                                               (19)

This is a point in the RELATIVE INTERIOR of Lvert U. Its horizontal
increment u is positive and smaller than N<4/3<b+1, and it lies
on the upper supporting line. The residual in(19) has positive
coefficient. Hence(14)--(15) check the remote vertex V and the
optimum as well as the two edge vertices. G4 is the actual gradient.
The physical step h4=ell gives

    X5=(x5,t v5).

Its violation of the LOWER supporting inequality is exactly t d5>0.
The lower normal is n_minus=(-rho t,-1). Its true projected candidate is

    G5=(j5,t w5),
    Y5=X5-G5=(-rho E5,-delta t E5)
                 =(t d5/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,
    f5=<X5,G5>-||G5||^2/2.
                                                               (20)

The definitions in(2) give rho j5+w5+e=0. Also -b<j5<1 by(7),(13).
Thus G5 lies in the relative interior of Lvert V, rather than being
an endpoint or an affine projection outside the segment. The positive
normal coefficient and(14) verify(15) against the REMOTE U as well.
This is the second genuine face transition, at an internal query of
the second original H2 block.

The next original step h5=1+beta gives

    X6=(x6,t v6).

By(13), x6<1, v6-x6<1/15<D, and rho x6+v6+e>0. Therefore
X6 is in the interior of T49, and G6=X6, f6=||X6||^2/2.
The original step h6=ell gives

    X7=-a X6.

For this point its horizontal coordinate is negative, and its upper
line expression divided by t is a(x6-v6)<1/3<D. Its lower slack,
also divided by t, is

    e-a(rho x6+v6)>9/10-(1/2)[(3/5)(2/3)+2/3]>0.

Thus G7=X7 and f7=||X7||^2/2. The physical bridge at index7 is
1+beta c^2. Consequently

    X8=-(beta c^2)X7=gamma X6=(x8,t v8).
                                                               (21)

Its horizontal coordinate is greater than3/2. Its projection is
NOT itself. Since3/2<v8<8/5<D+1 and the bottom endpoint of UV
has negative vertical coordinate, the exact projection is

    G8=(1,t v8), Y8=(x8-1,0),
    f8=<X8,G8>-||G8||^2/2.
                                                               (22)

It lies in the relative interior of UV, and the positive horizontal
residual proves(15) for the entire triangle, including Lvert and0.
The next original step h8=ell now gives

    X9=(x9,t v9).
                                                               (23)

In the second H2, G4 has positive vertical coordinate, G5 negative,
G6 positive, and G7 negative. The gradients vary at EVERY one of its
three actual updates. No constant-block compression has been used.

## 7. Permanent core from9 with every physical query retained

Both endpoints X9 and -c X9 lie in the strict interior of T49.
For X9, the right and upper inequalities in(14) follow from(13),
and its lower slack divided by t is greater than e-2/3>0.
For -c X9, the right inequality holds because its horizontal coordinate
is negative. Its upper expression divided by t satisfies

    c(x9-v9)<(5/2)(3/25+2/3)=59/30<D.

Its lower slack is

    e+c(-rho x9-v9)>e+c[3/5-(3/5)(3/25)]>0.

By convexity,

    [-c X9,X9] subset interior(T49).
                                                               (24)

Here [P,Q] denotes the closed Euclidean segment, including its endpoints.
For a positive integer j let nu2(j) be the exponent of2 dividing j,
and for a nonnegative integer i let s2(i) be the number of binary1
digits in i, with s2(0)=0. The original word recursion gives

    h_i=1+beta c^(nu2(i+1)-1), 0<=i<n.

Indeed the central position in each word has the stated valuation,
and adding its power of2 to a right-child index preserves the smaller
valuation of that child index. Induction on the recursion proves the
formula. Counting multiples of2^j gives

    sum_(j=1)^i nu2(j)=sum_(r>=1)floor(i/2^r)=i-s2(i).

The last equality follows by writing i in binary and summing the
contribution 2^r-1 of each binary1 digit. Thus the EXACT prefix product
on the original physical word is

    Pprod_i:=product_(j<i)(1-h_j)
             =(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)).
                                                               (25)

For EVERY9<=i<=n define

    sigma_i=(-1)^(i-9) beta^(i-9)c^(2-s2(i)),
    Xi=Gi=sigma_i X9, fi=||Xi||^2/2, Yi=0.
                                                               (26)

For odd i>=9, s2(i)>=2, so0<sigma_i<=1. For even i>=10,
s2(i)>=1, so-c<=sigma_i<0. Hence(24) proves every Xi in(26)
lies in T49 and is its own actual projection. Equation(25) proves
Xi+1=(1-h_i)Xi for every remaining physical step. This is induction
from the already established X9, not an assumed quadratic suffix.
All factors are nonzero, so the gradients continue to change sign at
every physical query, including all later original H2 blocks.

## 8. Every remote and optimum interpolation row

At every finite sample use the single value

    fi=<Xi,Gi>-||Gi||^2/2.

This agrees with(17),(19),(20),(22),(26) and the core values at6,7.
Put X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0, Y_star=0. Direct expansion of the
accepted convention gives, for every distinct pair of indices,

    Q_ij:=fi-fj+<Gi,Xj-Xi>+||Gi-Gj||^2/2
         =<Gi-Gj,Yj>.
                                                               (27)

Every Gi is in the SAME triangle. For j=0,1,2,3 and8, Yj is a
nonnegative right normal; for j=4 it is a positive upper normal;
for j=5 it is a positive lower normal. Equations(14) and(19)--(22)
therefore make(27) nonpositive for EVERY possible source i, including
all remote samples and the optimum. For j=6,7 and j>=9 it is zero.
For j=star it is also zero. Since G_star is in the triangle, the
reverse optimum inequalities are included too. In particular no
incoming or outgoing terminal row, earlier remote row, or original
query has been omitted.

Together with Sections5--7 this supplies a full static interpolant,
its true minimum set, all original points, gradients, values and
normal conditions. Dimension2 and the paid radius in(16) are separate
quantities. Orthogonal embedding preserves the same closest distance:
the minimizer set in the larger space is the orthogonal complement
of the original plane.

## 9. Two exact original-row obstructions

### 9.1 Continuing the lower affine face through query6

Retain all actual points, gradients and values through5. Define the
closest point to0 on the lower supporting LINE by

    q=(e t/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,
    w=G5-q.

Then q is perpendicular to w, and(20) gives

    X5=(1+d5/e)q+w,
    X6=(d5/e-beta)q-beta w.

The affine-line continuation would prescribe

    G6_aff=q-beta w,
    Y6_aff=[d5/e-(1+beta)]q.
                                                               (28)

By(7),(10), the scalar in square brackets is negative. Using the
natural projection-form value at6, direct expansion(27) gives

    Q_(0,6_aff)
       =(1+beta-d5/e) e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2)>20.
                                                               (29)

To see this exact numerator, U=G0 and

    <q,U-q>=-e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2).

This follows from <n_minus,U>-e t=-ell(D+1)t, using
e+rho=a(D+1). The candidate G6_aff lies on the same supporting
line as q, so <q,G6_aff>=||q||^2. Substitution into(28) proves
the equality in(29). For its quantitative bound, d5/e<10/207<1/20,
1+beta>19/10, ell>7/5, D+1>18/5, e>9/10, and
1/(rho^2+delta)>5/2. Their product is greater than
(37/20)(9/10)(7/5)(18/5)(5/2)=209790/10000>20.

Allowing a free value f6 cannot repair this prescribed gradient while
the prior value f5 is retained. In fact

    G5-G6_aff=(1+beta)w,
    X5-X6=(1+beta)G5,
    <G5-G6_aff,X5-X6>=||G5-G6_aff||^2.

Therefore Q_(5,6_aff)+Q_(6_aff,5)=0 independently of f6. If both
rows were nonpositive, both would be equalities and they would uniquely
force f6. The projection-form value used in(29) makes both equalities
hold, since both normals are perpendicular to w. Thus it is the forced
value and the original remote row(0,6) remains positive. This rejects
only this same-line continuation with the specified retained jets;
it does not reject arbitrary new gradients or a new whole transcript.

### 9.2 Declaring a permanent core at query6

Retain the ACTUAL construction through7, including its optimum and
initial jet. The physical query X8 is fixed by(21). The false quadratic
continuation would set G8_core=X8. The two optimum rows in accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 force f8_core=||X8||^2/2. Since Y0=(A-1,0),

    Q_(8_core,0)=(A-1)(x8-1)>(A-1)/2>2.
                                                               (30)

Thus even with free new values the stated core continuation is
impossible for ANY smooth convex interpolant retaining these earlier
jets and optimum. The actual projection(22) repairs this row by
returning to the old vertical face. Both source and target in(30)
are original physical queries, not added support points or compressed
block endpoints.

## 10. Exact true-distance payoff

Since n=2^m-1 is odd and s2(n)=m, formulas(16),(26) yield the exact
normalized payoff of this actual function:

    V49(m,t):=2(F49(Xn)-min F49)/dist(X0,argmin F49)^2
      =beta^(2(n-9)) c^(4-2m)
             (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)>0.
                                                               (31)

Let Qunit=Pprod_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m). This is the scalar unit
quadratic's exact payoff on the SAME word. Using t>=1 and(7),(13),

    (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)
       <[(3/25)^2+(2/3)^2]/(18/5)^2
        =2581/72900<1/25.

Bernoulli's inequality beta^18>=(1-18/m)>=9/10 follows by induction
from (1-v)^r>=1-rv for0<=v<=1. Thus

    V49/Qunit<beta^(-18)c^4/25
              <(10/9)(5/2)^4/25=125/72<2.
                                                               (32)

The bound by2 Qunit has a precise large-aspect limit. Fix any allowed
m and let t tend to infinity, so delta tends to0 while the enclosing
word stays fixed. All coefficients in(1)--(2) remain finite and have
limits. At4 the projected vertical coefficient tends to A s; at5
it tends to -a A s because delta E5 tends to0. Hence successive
vertical coefficients give

    lim_(t->infinity) v6=a beta A s,
    lim_(t->infinity) v8=beta^4 A s,
    lim_(t->infinity) v9=-a beta^4 A s
                       =(D+1)Pprod_9.

The last identity uses D+1=k s=A s/(a beta^3) and
Pprod_9=-beta^9/c^2. Divide numerator and denominator of the radius
ratio in(31) by t^2. Since x9 and A remain bounded, it follows that

    lim_(t->infinity) V49(m,t)/Qunit(m)=1.                (32a)

Thus arbitrarily increasing the aspect does not improve its asymptotic
scale, even though the same three-face pattern remains present at
every finite allowed t. No claim that V49<=Qunit at every finite t
is needed or asserted.

The payoff is paid at the closest actual minimizer by(16). For any
positive smoothness constant L_s, replace F49 by L_s F49 and use
steps h_i/L_s. All positions, closest distances and normalized payoffs
stay the same. The convention is W=2R throughout.

## 11. Complete same-word references and original B_n

Put S=sum H_m and M=1+S. The exact multiplicities of the recursively
defined steps give

    M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)
       =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta c^m,
    zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).
                                                               (33)

Indeed summing the unit portions gives2^m-1, and summing the excesses
gives (beta/c)sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(m-1-j)c^j. The geometric sum is
the displayed quotient. Since c>lambda>2 and lambda(lambda-2)=1,
one has c(c-2)>1>beta. Consequently M<c^m.

The COMPLETE reference from accepted b98bb807d514e80d is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
      max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/
         [(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]}.

Define Huber=1/(1+2S)=1/(2M-1). The complete F_ray_n of accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a maximizes over ALL feasible chronological checkpoint
sets, retaining the offsets and accumulated value areas specified
there; in particular its zero-cut option is Huber and its one-cut
options are all the displayed K terms. Thus Huber<=K_n<=F_ray_n,
with no checkpoint choice suppressed.

Using(32)--(33), beta<1 and c>2,

    V49/Huber=V49(2M-1)<4 c^(-m)<1/10.
                                                               (34)

The final strict inequality already follows for m>=6 because2^6>40;
our stated domain has m>=1000000. This proves the full K/ray comparison.
It uses a lower bound on each complete reference, rather than replacing
the reference by a chosen checkpoint.

For completeness, the FULL complex reference in accepted
dfde2fa326c908bf uses Poly_i(z)=product_(j<i)(1-h_j z), the real-plane
points xi_i=Poly_i(z), gradients gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), and the optimum0.
For every directed simple cycle C on{star,0,...,n}, it imposes

    (Re(z)-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|Im(z) Area_C|,
    Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2,
    Area_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).

D_h is the set satisfying ALL these cycles. Gap_h(z) is the maximal
normalized terminal objective among interpolants of these specified
points and gradients with f(0)=0, using initial point1 and denominator1.
We compare with the entire supremum over D_h.

Take the real parameter theta=1/(2M). It is in[0,1], so all cycle
inequalities hold: their right sides vanish and their left coefficients
are nonnegative. The explicit convex1-smooth scalar quadratic
f_theta(x)=theta x^2/2 produces the exact specified real polynomial
points and gradients. It can be embedded in the real plane by making
the function independent of the second coordinate. Its minimum is0
and its initial squared distance is1. Therefore the maximality in the
accepted fact implies

    Gap_h(theta)>=theta Poly_n(theta)^2.

All factors are positive and
sum_j theta h_j=S/(2M)<1/2. Induction on the factors, using
(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v for u,v>=0, proves

    Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta S>1/2,
    sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)>1/(8M)>1/(8c^m).
                                                               (35)

Together with(32),

    V49/[sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)]<16 c^(-m)<1/10.
                                                               (36)

The final inequality follows for m>=8 because2^8>160. This is a
comparison to the FULL unaugmented complex channel, not only its
universal lens. It also dominates V49 after any Huber augmentation.

Finally retain the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences. In the notation of
accepted b98bb807d514e80d they are Cbench_0=B_0=1 and

    Cbench_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(Cbench_j,Cbench_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gjoin(Cbench_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    Gjoin(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2.

Cbench is only a local alias for the accepted primitive mass. Using
its proved bound B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<5*3^m at n=2^m-1, and(32),

    0<B_n V49<10(3/c^2)^m<10(25/48)^m ->0.
                                                               (37)

This closes the entire stated large-aspect family uniformly, even if
t grows arbitrarily fast with m. It is not an upper bound on W_n(h),
and does not transfer the finite accepted H6 result to another word.

## 12. Exact remaining boundaries

The first new conclusion is a complete legal three-face construction
for every stated(m,t), with changing gradients inside physical H2
blocks, every ordered original row, and true minimum/radius accounting.
It enters the core permanently only AFTER the remote-forced return8.
Its payoff is quantitatively below every complete reference above.

The two row obstructions(29)--(30) retain specific earlier jets and
the optimum. They close exactly the corresponding proposed next
gradients, including arbitrary reassigned new values. They do not
exclude whole-transcript redesign, different extra vertices, conjugate
heights, a moved initial state, or a changing closest-minimum state.

Together with max48 as awareness only, the two drafts identify a
balanced-aspect failure and the present large-aspect failure. This
joint draft alone does NOT close the intermediate/small aspect region,
all triangles, all polytopes, or all changing-gradient laws. It does
not assert optimality of its phase partition outside its stated domain.

A stronger family on these H_m words would still leave the original
all-n/all-nonnegative-word lower comparison BLOCKED. That is a
theorem-level requirement, not a routine induction. No actual seed
for the separate verified upper-lift interface is supplied either.

Resource record: zero new host mathematical evaluations, numerical
solves, DP, trajectory runs, proof replays, machine algebra, formal
verifier calls, fact_submit calls, installations, consults or native
subagents. Literature retrieval, file reads and textual persistence
only. All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED. No further round or
formal gate is renewed by this handoff.

# max65: completed full-objective discovery for the exact small-tau hierarchy

**Status: numerical discovery complete; no new fact; original minimax goal OPEN.**
All six authorized jobs completed, with five solver invocations and one
independent solver-free readback. The canonical ledger is `closed_exhausted`:
180 accounted seconds, **49.26249355098116 measured external child seconds**,
zero remaining jobs. No correction, timeout, failed mathematical job,
installation, external service, subagent, new benchmark DP, or formal
submission was used. The reservation guard1131->1311 is not measured runtime.

The finite full-PEP results are numerically Huber-like on all four specified
words. None of these solves supplies a stronger numerical objective than
the same-word Huber witness. This batch did not find the extra hard geometry
sought by the assignment. This is a scoped discovery outcome, not proof of
Huber optimality, a worst-case upper bound, or a conclusion about large m.

## 1. Exact object and prior-work distinction

For each enclosing m in {3,4,5,6}, the word is exactly

    tau=1/4, lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta_word/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

All children retain their enclosing m and tau. The numerical records save
the binary64 evaluation of these formulas and every physical step.

The historical full-PEP finding df56220f87e79c22 uses tau=1 at each of
m=3,4,5,6. This was checked from every public raw schedule formula, not
from an H label or a search match. Accepted e9a55f57461f253c uses exactly
the old m=6,tau=1 word, with c=(7/6)lambda and a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7.
The new word differs already at its first leaf: the factor
(1-tau/m)/(1+tau/m) decreases strictly with tau, by cross multiplication.
Thus the previous experiments were not a replay of the new cases.
`historical_identity_inputs.json` stores the source identities and hashes.

Current steering: assignment/master_guidance6a33fa593da4ce7d; the later
7a4e289802c5f694 expressly leaves this max65 allocation unchanged.
Preregistration finding:79fdc29308070926. The three code files, exact saved
benchmark rows, historical identity record and preregistration were hashed
before the first mathematical job in `freeze_manifest.json`.

## 2. Full original mathematical model

Normalize L=1 and the SPECIFIED minimizer x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The
full symmetric Gram matrix G is on all formal vectors(x0,g0,...,gn).
The physical positions are x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. The program maximizes
2f_n under

    G PSD; G00=1;
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0
    for all distinct ordered i,j in {star,0,...,n}.

Diagonal rows are identities0=0. Every original nontrivial row is retained,
including both terminal directions and both minimizer directions. The
terminal gradient g_n is an interpolation variable, not an extra update.
There is no rank, support, scalar, quadratic, or primitive-certificate
restriction. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 proof Sections1--3 supplies the full
interpolation equivalence and attained normalization at G00=1.

The only conditioning is the invertible transformation
M=1+sum h, D=diag(1,1/M,...,1/M), G=D Ghat D, f=fhat/M. The solver maximizes
2fhat_n and imposes M Q<=0. If A,F are the original Gram/value row maps,
the raw pair multipliers lambda are already in original units, while
q=raw_norm_multiplier/M. The full original dual quadratic matrix is
C=q E00+A^T lambda. Its original value flow must be F^T lambda=2e_n,
and scaled stationarity compares M D C D with the raw PSD multiplier.
The norm multiplier is unrestricted because normalization is equality.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| h=(3/2) | 1 | 3 | 6 | 8 | 17 |
| H_(3,1/4) | 7 | 9 | 72 | 53 | 13 |
| H_(4,1/4) | 15 | 17 | 272 | 169 | 14 |
| H_(5,1/4) | 31 | 33 | 1056 | 593 | 20 |
| H_(6,1/4) | 63 | 65 | 4160 | 2209 | 29 |

All five solver statuses were `optimal`, using the fixed settings
CLARABEL(max_iter120,max_threads1,time_limit20,tol_gap_abs1e-9,
tol_gap_rel1e-9,tol_feas1e-9). An independent external30-second timeout
covered each entire child, including numerical imports, build, solve,
all diagnostics and raw export. No retry or fallback occurred.

Runtime: Python3.12.14, CVXPY1.9.3, CLARABEL0.11.1, NumPy2.5.3,
SciPy1.18.1; arithmetic IEEE754 binary64. Solver tolerance is not arithmetic
precision. BLAS and solver thread counts were restricted to1.

## 3. Calibration with exact symbolic reference

For h=(3/2), expand the original Q rows to obtain

 (1/4)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
        =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2.

Thus2f1<=1/4 for G00=1. The scalar function f(x)=x^2/2 with x0=1 gives
x1=-1/2 and2f1=1/4. The Huber function with threshold1/4 also attains

preregistration before execution, not a post-fit interpretation.

The solver returned primal0.24999999998853667 and dual0.24999999993770056,

was-5.248615431163728e-11. All frozen calibration checks passed.

## 4. Saved full-objective results and comparison directions

# Every finite delay: exact value pricing and complete redundancy

Status: UNVERIFIED complete joint proof draft, max2 round26.
TASK.md and master_guidance6e0a18d700dcd62f prohibit fact_submit.
Nothing in this document is an accepted fact. No numerical/model scan,
solver, machine algebra, replay, installation or subordinate agent was used.

Accepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750 Section10 for the ORIGINAL
primitive identity and its full formal-gradient certificate interface;
fact1252b8823bb5a290 proof paragraphs1--2 for the interpolation convention
and constructive sufficiency. Every other calculation is proved jointly
below. The earlier immediate-annihilation draft cc0fc35f2fb5d6bb is
awareness, not a predecessor or an assumed lemma.

Sections1--7 first prove a family with TWO negative fast samples before
annihilation, including its exact three-path price and a polynomial
budget. Section9 then proves the stronger final result: ANY finite
negative excursion after a reversal at ANY index, followed by
annihilation, is redundant under the stated global and local prefix
bounds. For killing step b<=2 the local bounds are automatic. Thus this
final scope is not limited to an initial reversal, two negative samples,
or the previous immediately annihilated family. Its entire
parameter-and-value minimum is decided, not merely its price formula.

The proof supplies one static attaining smooth convex function, every
directed finite and optimum inequality, the complete minimizer set and
true distance, all coincident-point boundaries, and all zero-step indices.
It does not decide the general full cone or the original exact-profile
target. The verifier gate stays closed.

## 1. Family, exact minimum and complete comparison

Fix an integer n>=3 and a nonnegative finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)).
Write P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_n and C=1+s. Assume

    h_0=r, h_1=c, h_2=b,
    1<=b<=r<=2, 0<=c<=b,
    h_i<=P_i+2 for every i<n.                       (1)

No bound on n or on the total tail mass is imposed. In particular the
case n=4 retains the original five formal gradients at arbitrary
capacity allowed by(1); capacity C is not frozen to a sample value.

Choose d>=1, amplitudes u_1,...,u_d,v>=0 and curvatures satisfying

    beta=1/b, 0<alpha_q<beta,
    alpha_q*h_i<=1 for every i<n and 1<=q<=d.

Set

    p_(0,q)=1, p_(i,q)=product_(t<i)(1-alpha_q*h_t),
    m=r/b-1, theta=1-c/b,
    z_0=1, z_1=-m, z_2=-m*theta, z_i=0 for i>=3,
    x_i=(u_q*p_(i,q) for1<=q<=d, v*z_i),
    g_i=(alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q) for1<=q<=d, beta*v*z_i). (2)

These samples obey every GD update x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. Before values
are varied they are samples of the SINGLE fixed diagonal quadratic

    F_base(x)=sum_q alpha_q*x_q^2/2+beta*x_(d+1)^2/2.

Thus no Hessian switching or gluing of independently chosen jets is
assumed. Every slow coordinate is nonnegative and nonincreasing. If
r>b, v>0 and 0<c<b, the fast mode is positive at0, negative and nonzero
at1 and2, and first zero at3. This is genuine delayed annihilation.

Define the nonnegative slack weights and the first three slow gaps by

    U_q=alpha_q*(1-alpha_q)*u_q^2/2,
    B=beta*(1-beta)*v^2/2,
    Delta_(i,q)=p_(i,q)-p_(i+1,q) for0<=i<n.

The three energy price increments are

    X26=4r*sum_q U_q*(Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                     +Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(2,q)
                     +Delta_(1,q)*Delta_(2,q)),
    Z26=4r*[sum_q U_q*Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                         +B*m*theta*(1+m*theta)],
    Y26=4r*B*[m+m^2*(1-theta+theta^2)].             (3)

Here X26 bypasses BOTH negative samples; Z26 visits the second negative
sample and bypasses the first; Y26 visits both chronologically in reverse.
They are increments above the sum of separate-mode optimal prices.

For a_(i,q)=alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q) and
t_(i,q)=a_(i,q)-a_(i+1,q), put

    L_slow=sum_(q,i<n) P_(i+1)*t_(i,q)
               *[2(1+P_i)*a_(i,q)-P_(i+1)*t_(i,q)],
    K_neg=c^2*beta*(2-c^2*beta)
                   +(c+b)*(c+2-b)*theta^2,
    H_fast=beta^2*v^2*[r*(2-r)+m^2*K_neg].          (4)

For any scalar values f_i on(2), define the primitive energy

    V_h(f)=2sum_(i<n)h_i*(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i*(1-h_i)*||g_i||^2-s*(s+1)*||g_n||^2.

Among ALL values satisfying ALL ordered finite interpolation constraints,
the exact minimum is

    V_min=L_slow+H_fast-min(X26,Y26,Z26).           (5)

The same minimum holds when the fixed actual optimum jet(0,0,0) is
required. It is attained by the static function in Section6.

The full parameter comparison is

    L_slow>=X26+r^2*(2-r)*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2,
    H_fast>=beta^2*v^2*r*(2-r),
    V_min>=r*(2-r)*[beta^2*v^2
                           +r*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2]>=0. (6)

The first THREE terms of each slow-mode energy already cover its share
of X26. All subsequent terms are nonnegative. The comparison is uniform
over every permitted curvature, amplitude, tail and choice of legal
joint values, including values from nonseparable functions. Consequently
the ENTIRE displayed delayed-annihilation family imposes no additional
primitive condition beyond(1).

For r<2 and a nonzero initial vector, the final bound in(6) is strict.
For 1<b<r, 0<c<b, v>0 and at least one active slow mode with
alpha_q*r<1, all three quantities in(3) are positive. Thus incompatible
orderings really do create a strict positive joint price. Inequality(6)
decides its information content after full optimization.

## 2. Original orientation and the all-value problem

Keep every original index I={0,...,n}, including zero-step samples.
For distinct i,j set

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 gives both the necessity of Q_ij<=0 and its
constructive sufficiency with an optimum sample. Let Lambda_ij>=0,
Lambda_ii=0, e=(1,...,1)^T, and define

    L_flow=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    D_prim=diag(h_i*(2-h_i) for i<n, -s*(s+2)).

The ORIGINAL sufficient primitive certificate is

    L_flow^T*e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T+T^T*L_flow^T)/2 PSD on R^(n+1). (7)

Its full identity from accepted04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h(f)+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij*Q_ij
                        =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>. (8)

The flow here is OUTGOING minus incoming. The terminal n is the source
of mass2s, and each i<n is a sink of mass2h_i. An auxiliary minimum-cost
flow below is not presumed to satisfy the PSD condition in(7).

For the base values f_i^0=F_base(x_i), direct expansion gives the
symmetric nonnegative slack costs

    w_ij=-Q_ij(f^0)
        =sum_q U_q*(p_(i,q)-p_(j,q))^2+B*(z_i-z_j)^2. (9)

Let d_i be the shortest path cost n->i in the complete finite graph
with these costs. The minimum exists over simple paths; nonnegative
costs allow the removal of every cycle even when some edges cost zero.
New values f_i^0+delta_i satisfy every directed inequality exactly when

    delta_i-delta_j<=w_ij for every distinct i,j.

Every path n->i implies delta_i-delta_n>=-d_i. Since h_i>=0,

    min_f V_h(f)=V_h(f^0)-2sum_(i<n)h_i*d_i.       (10)

All lower bounds are attained simultaneously by delta_i=-d_i: the
distance inequalities d_j<=d_i+w_ij verify EVERY directed constraint.

For any original prescribed flow, w_ij>=d_j-d_i and the flow equation
give

    sum Lambda_ij*w_ij>=-sum omega_i*d_i
                                =2sum_(i<n)h_i*d_i.

Send2h_i units along a shortest n->i path for each i with h_i>0.
This is a nonnegative original prescribed flow attaining equality.
Zero-weight vertices remain in the complete graph. This proves that
(10) is the exact ORIGINAL terminal-source price, not the reverse-flow
quantity. It also proves why this optimizing flow need not be PSD.

## 3. All shortest paths and the three competing increments

For ordered scalar positions p_0>=...>=p_n and squared-distance edge
costs U*(p_i-p_j)^2, the distance from n to i is

    U*sum_(k=i)^(n-1)(p_k-p_(k+1))^2.              (11)

Indeed an edge spanning several ordered gaps has cost at least the sum
of the squared gaps it spans. Every path between two ordered positions
crosses every intervening gap at least once. Summing these lower bounds
proves(11); the path through the consecutive ordered points attains it.
Zero gaps and tied positions satisfy the same proof.

In the separate fast scalar coordinate, the finite sampled positions
are1,-m,-m*theta,0 with possible repetitions. The shortest costs from
the zero terminal are

    d_0^fast=B,
    d_1^fast=B*m^2*[theta^2+(1-theta)^2],
    d_2^fast=B*m^2*theta^2,
    d_i^fast=0 for i>=3.                           (12)

Apply the same ordered-gap proof on each side of0. For the positive
target1, detouring through any negative position only increases cost.
For each negative target, detouring through the positive position only
increases cost. This also proves every equality in degenerate cases.

Let d_i^sep be the sum of(12) and all slow distances(11). Every joint
path costs at least the sum of the separate minima. For every i>=1,
the chronological path n,n-1,...,i simultaneously attains all those
separate minima, so

    d_i=d_i^sep for every i>=1.                    (13)

There is no assumption that the best paths to0 coincide across modes.
For a path whose last edge is k->0, the preceding cost is at least
d_k^sep, and this lower bound is attained by a path avoiding0.
Thus d_0=min_(k>=1)(d_k^sep+w_(k,0)).

Among k>=3 the best choice is k=3. The fast position is zero at every
such k. In each slow coordinate insert the intermediate points
k-1,...,3 into the last edge k->0. Splitting a nonnegative gap reduces
the sum of squared gaps, so

    d_k^sep+w_(k,0)>=d_3^sep+w_(3,0).

Therefore exactly three paths suffice: n,...,3,0; n,...,2,0; and
n,...,1,0. Their costs minus d_0^sep are respectively

    2sum_q U_q*(Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                +Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(2,q)
                +Delta_(1,q)*Delta_(2,q)),
    2sum_q U_q*Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                         +2B*m*theta*(1+m*theta),
    2B*[m+m^2*(1-theta+theta^2)].                  (14)

For the first line expand the square of the sum of three slow gaps.
For the second line the slow excess is the cross product of the first
two gaps; the fast excess is
B*[(m*theta)^2+(1+m*theta)^2-1]. For the third line the fast excess is
B*[m^2*theta^2+m^2*(1-theta)^2+(1+m)^2-1]. These expansions give(14).

Equations(13)--(14) check all possible paths in the COMPLETE graph.
In particular, no detour via a later vertex or via the opposite sign
can evade them. By the distance argument of Section2, all directed
inequalities for the optimal potential follow. Multiplication of(14)
by2h_0=2r proves the exact joint price increment

    2sum_(i<n)h_i*(d_i-d_i^sep)=min(X26,Y26,Z26).  (15)

An attaining original flow sends the sink at0 along the least-cost
one of these three paths, and every other sink chronologically. Ties
can be resolved either way. All flows have the original source and
exact node divergences, and no PSD claim is made for them.

## 4. Exact separate energies

For a slow scalar coordinate write a_i=alpha*u*p_i and
t_i=a_i-a_(i+1)>=0. At its optimal scalar values the reverse adjacent
inequality is tight and

    f_i-f_(i+1)=h_i*a_i*a_(i+1)+t_i^2/2.

This follows either by substituting the base values minus distances(11),
or by expanding that tight inequality. Substituting these differences
into V_h makes its value term

    sum_i[2P_(i+1)*h_i*a_i*a_(i+1)+P_(i+1)*t_i^2].

After writing a_(i+1)=a_i-t_i, the coefficient of a_i^2 is
h_i*(1+2P_i+h_i)=P_(i+1)*(P_(i+1)+1)-P_i*(P_i+1).
Telescope those coefficients against the terminal term. The result is

    L_alpha=sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)*t_i
                  *[2(1+P_i)*a_i-P_(i+1)*t_i].    (16)

This is an EXACT scalar minimum since the distance potential satisfies
every nonadjacent inequality as well. Also 0<=t_i<=a_i, so

    2(1+P_i)*a_i-P_(i+1)*t_i
                   >=(P_i+2-h_i)*a_i>=0.          (17)

Thus every slow energy summand is nonnegative under(1).

The separate fast optimum has terminal value0 and f_0=beta^2*v^2/2.
On its negative side, the two-step word(c,b) starts at gradient
-beta*v*m, contracts by theta at its first step and reaches zero at
its second. Sign reflection does not change its energy. Formula(16),
proved directly above, on this two-step monotone history gives

    beta^2*v^2*m^2*
       [c^2*beta*(2-c^2*beta)+(c+b)*(c+2-b)*theta^2].

The initial positive sample contributes beta^2*v^2*r*(2-r), with
no nonzero terminal term because the final fast gradient is zero.
This proves the exact H_fast in(4). Since c<=b<=2,

    2-c^2*beta>=2-c>=0, c+2-b>=0,

so K_neg>=0. The base energy is a sum of coordinate energies. Combining
their exact separate minima with(15) proves the exact joint minimum(5).
It has not combined incompatible scalar values into a supposed function;
its actual simultaneous values are constructed in Section6.

## 5. Three slow terms pay for the full delayed bypass

It suffices to consider one slow mode alpha,u. Write

    ell=1-r*alpha, tau=c/r, zeta=b/r,
    a=1-tau, k=a+tau*ell, R=1+tau+zeta.

All ell,tau,zeta belong to[0,1], and p_1=ell, p_2=ell*k.
Let X26_alpha be that coordinate's contribution to X26. Since
Delta_0=r*alpha, Delta_1=c*alpha*ell and
Delta_2=b*alpha*ell*k, one has

    X26_alpha=2r*alpha^3*(1-alpha)*u^2
                       *[r*c*ell+r*b*ell*k+c*b*ell^2*k].

The first three terms of(16), divided by alpha^3*u^2 when u>0, are

    r^2*(2-r^2*alpha)
    +c*(r+c)*ell^2*[2(1+r)-c*(r+c)*alpha]
    +b*(r+c+b)*ell^2*k^2*[2(1+r+c)-b*(r+c+b)*alpha].

Define S=tau*ell+zeta*ell*k+tau*zeta*ell^2*k. Subtracting
X26_alpha and substituting alpha=(1-ell)/r gives exactly

    first_three_terms-X26_alpha=r^2*alpha^3*u^2*D(r),

    D(r)=2-r+r*ell
      +tau*(1+tau)*ell^2*[2+2r-r*tau*(1+tau)*(1-ell)]
      +zeta*R*ell^2*k^2*[2+2r*(1+tau)-r*zeta*R*(1-ell)]
      -2*(r-1+ell)*S.                             (18)

Here ell,tau,zeta are held fixed when viewing D(r) as an affine
polynomial in the symbol r. Its formal coefficient evaluation at r=0
is ONLY an algebraic device, not a proposed zero-step physical instance:

    D(0)=2+2tau*(1+tau)*ell^2+2zeta*R*ell^2*k^2
                                              +2(1-ell)*S>=2. (19)

At r=2, put A=6-2tau-2tau^2 and B_aux=6+4tau-2zeta*R. Since
zeta<=1, zeta*R<=2+tau and B_aux>=2+2tau. Expanding(18) gives

    D(2)=C_1*ell+C_2*ell^2+C_3*ell^3+C_4*ell^4+C_5*ell^5,
    C_1=2a*(1-zeta),
    C_2=tau*(1+tau)*A+zeta*R*a^2*B_aux
                    -2[tau+zeta*a+tau*zeta*(2-tau)],
    C_3=2tau^2*(1+tau)^2+2zeta*R*a*tau*B_aux
                         +2zeta^2*R^2*a^2-4tau*zeta,
    C_4=tau^2*zeta*(R*B_aux-2)+4zeta^2*R^2*a*tau,
    C_5=2zeta^2*R^2*tau^2.                        (20)

C_1,C_4,C_5 are nonnegative: a>=0, zeta<=1, R>=1 and B_aux>=2.
The two remaining coefficients have explicit nonnegative bounds.
First define F_tau=tau*(2+2tau-2tau^2-tau^3). Then

    C_2/2
      =F_tau-zeta*(1+tau-tau^2)
                +zeta*R*a^2*(3+2tau-zeta*R)
      >=F_tau-zeta*tau*(1+tau-tau^3)
                                      +zeta^2*(1+tau)*a^2
      >=tau*(1-tau)*(1+2tau)+zeta^2*(1+tau)*a^2>=0. (21)

For the first inequality use 3+2tau-zeta*R>=1+tau and
(1+tau)^2*a^2=(1-tau^2)^2. For the second use zeta<=1 and
tau*(1+tau-tau^3)>=0, then expand F_tau-tau*(1+tau-tau^3).
For C_3 use B_aux>=2(1+tau) to obtain

    C_3/2>=[tau*(1+tau)+zeta*R*a]^2-2tau*zeta.

The expression inside the square exceeds tau+zeta because

    tau*(1+tau)+zeta*R*a-(tau+zeta)
                         =tau^2*(1-zeta)+zeta^2*a>=0.

All quantities being squared are nonnegative. Hence

    C_3/2>=(tau+zeta)^2-2tau*zeta=tau^2+zeta^2>=0. (22)

Every coefficient in(20) is nonnegative, proving D(2)>=0. Affineness
and 0<=r<=2 now give

    D(r)=(1-r/2)*D(0)+(r/2)*D(2)>=2-r.            (23)

Multiply back in(18). If u=0, the same multiplied identity holds
without division. Every later term in(16) is nonnegative by(17).
Therefore

    L_alpha>=X26_alpha+r^2*(2-r)*alpha^3*u^2.

Sum over all slow modes, use min(X26,Y26,Z26)<=X26, and combine with
the fast lower bound from Section4. This proves ALL of(6), including
the entire parameter minimization. No numerical coefficient checks or
symbolic-algebra computation have been substituted for this proof.

## 6. Static attainment, fixed optimum and true distance

Append a distinguished index star with x_star=g_star=f_star=0.
Its shortest distance from n is

    D_star=sum_q U_q*p_(n,q)^2.                    (24)

Indeed the fast terminal is zero, and in each slow coordinate every
finite position is at least its terminal position. No sampled position
lies strictly between zero and that terminal position. The ordered-gap
proof of(11) therefore gives a lower bound U_q*p_(n,q)^2 for each
slow coordinate. The direct edge to star attains their sum.

Adding star cannot shorten any finite distance. For every finite j,

    w_(n,j)=D_star+w_(star,j)
                        -2sum_q U_q*p_(n,q)*p_(j,q)
             <=D_star+w_(star,j).

Any path through star first pays at least D_star to reach it. Replace
that prefix and its following edge star->j by n->j. The cost does not
increase. Thus the finite distances in Section3 remain exact after
adding the optimum. No optimum multiplier has been inserted into(7).

The simultaneous attaining values are

    f_i=F_base(x_i)+D_star-d_i, f_star=0.          (25)

They are the negative distance potential shifted by D_star relative
to the base quadratic. All ordered finite and optimum inequalities
follow from distance inequalities, in BOTH directions, including all
coincident-point pairs.

For i in I together with star put y_i=x_i-g_i,
eta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f_i, including y_star=eta_star=0. Define on R^(d+1)

    H(w)=||w||^2/2+max_i(<y_i,w>+eta_i),
    F(x)=sup_w(<x,w>-H(w)).                        (26)

This is ONE static global function. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 proves
the needed constructive interpolation statement, and its elementary
argument applies to this complete finite set: H is finite, continuous,
1-strongly convex and has quadratic growth, so every x has a unique
maximizing w(x). Strong subgradient inequalities give
<x-x',w(x)-w(x')>>=||w(x)-w(x')||^2. The maximizing inequalities
then prove differentiability of F with gradient w(x), which is
1-Lipschitz. As a supremum of affine functions, F is convex.

At each g_i, expansion of a competing branch minus the i-th branch
is exactly Q_ij. All are nonpositive, and the i-th branch is active
with gradient x_i. Thus grad F(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i. In particular
F realizes EVERY GD step in(2), F(0)=0, and grad F(0)=0.

Its COMPLETE minimizer set and exact initial radius are

    K_min=conv{y_i:eta_i=0},
    R_true=dist(x_0,K_min).                        (27)

To verify this, the optimum inequalities give eta_i<=0 and eta_star=0.
The zero gradient condition is x in partial H(0). The subdifferential
there is the convex hull of the active affine slopes: a convex
combination gives the subgradient inequality directly; for a point
outside the hull, a separating direction and a sufficiently small
positive displacement exclude every inactive branch and contradict
that inequality. This proves both inclusions and(27).

If some active slow mode has p_(n,q)>0, all finite eta_i are strictly
negative. In fact d_i<=w_(n,i) and(25) give

    eta_i<=-2sum_q U_q*p_(i,q)*p_(n,q)<0.

Then K_min={0} and R_true^2=sum_q u_q^2+v^2. The complete open family
with alpha_q*h_i<1 and an active slow mode has this simpler radius.
If all slow amplitudes vanish and v>0, the largest active scalar slope
is y_0=(1-beta)*v, while all other slopes are nonpositive. Thus the
closest minimizer to x_0=v is y_0 and R_true=beta*v. Formula(27)
covers every other terminal-zero or vanishing-amplitude boundary
without asserting a false unique minimizer. For every nonzero initial
vector, g_0!=0, so x_0 is outside K_min and R_true>0.

The function can be rescaled to true initial radius1 by
F_scaled(z)=[F(x_closest+R_true*z)-min F]/R_true^2, where x_closest
is the Euclidean projection of x_0 onto K_min. Its gradient Lipschitz
constant remains1 and its GD uses the same word. Multiplication by
any L>0 gives the corresponding L-smooth instance.

## 7. Boundaries, prior tests and exact four-step scope

The formulas retain all non-strict boundaries:

* c=0 gives theta=1 and coincident samples1,2. Then Delta_1=0 and
  Y26=Z26; the theorem reduces algebraically to the earlier two-sample
  chronology after retaining its zero step.
* c=b gives theta=0, so the fast mode already reaches zero at2.
  Z26 is the shorter bypass through that first zero; the stated
  three-candidate minimum still includes it. Nothing assumes two
  strictly negative samples on this boundary.
* r=b or v=0 makes the genuine fast excursion absent and Y26=0.
* b=1 gives beta=1 and B=0, so Y26=0 as required by zero fast slacks.
* Vanishing slow amplitudes remove their costs and X26=0. If a slow
  product reaches zero, every subsequent gap of that mode is zero.
* r=2 is a nonnegative, not universally strictly positive, conclusion.
  Formula(23) still applies. No strict lower bound is falsely retained.
* Repeated points force equal gradients and equal attained values;
  their zero-cost paths verify both ordered interpolation inequalities.

Any additional zero steps inserted into the physical chronology can be
retained as repeated vertices with zero cost. The preceding path and
value proof applies with them present. No equality of FORMAL gradient
coordinates is imposed on the certificate(7), and no formal pair is
deleted. In n=4 the statement preserves all twenty original finite
multiplier signs and the entire ambient R^5 PSD requirement.

For clarity, the prefix-step bounds used in(1) are already necessary
for any primitive certificate. A direct legal witness proves this:
fix a cut j, put P=P_j, and suppose h_j>P+2. Take the one-sided capped
quadratic phi(t)=0 for t<=0, phi(t)=t^2/2 for0<=t<=1, and
phi(t)=t-1/2 for t>=1. It is convex differentiable and1-smooth, with
minimizers(-infinity,0]. Start at x_0=P+1. All gradients through j
are1; at j the point is1; after its step the point is1-h_j<0 and
all subsequent gradients and values are0. Substitution in V_h gives

    V_h=(P+h_j)*(P+2-h_j)<0.

Indeed for S=P+h_j, the value and gradient sums are
2(P+1)*S-2sum_(i<=j)h_i*P_i-sum_(i<=j)h_i^2
=2(P+1)*S-S^2. Its true initial distance is P+1. Identity(8) forbids
a full PSD certificate for this word. Thus h_i<=P_i+2 are old actual
primitive tests, not newly invented restrictions masking the gain.

In the four-step case the NEW interior covered here is

    h=(r,c,b,t), 1<b<r<=2, 0<c<b,
    0<=t<=r+c+b+2,
    0<alpha_q<min{1/b,1/max_i h_i}, beta=1/b.

The fast trajectory has two negative samples before the zero at3.
Tail t can be arbitrary in its displayed old-bound interval. The
complete redundancy is uniform over capacity C=1+r+c+b+t, including
C>=193/25 where that interval permits it. The theorem does not turn
any auxiliary minimum-price flow into a feasible full PSD matrix.

The finite-delay and initial-reversal restrictions of Sections1--7 are
removed in Section9 under its stated suffix hypotheses. Fast modes that
never reach zero, multiple sign reversals, multiple sign-changing
curvatures, and planar fractional repairs remain outside the result.
The complementary C82 full-system comparison remains open. No global
no-seed statement or original exact-profile answer follows.

## 8. Literature and verification status

The exact Matlas query about matched-curvature value minimization and
shortest squared-distance paths returned unrelated saddle-point and
low-rank-matrix statements; none was used. A primary-source web search
recovered Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, "Smooth Strongly Convex
Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods",
arXiv:1502.05666. The local extracted Theorem4 proof, constructive
Remark2, Corollary1 and cycle Remark3 were read. Their interpolation
mechanism is already proved in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, which is the
input actually used here; no additional external theorem is imported.

This is a complete joint DRAFT proof with exact new class scope and
full comparison. It remains UNVERIFIED. No intermediate draft is used
as an accepted brick, and no fact_submit has been made.

## 9. Arbitrary finite delay after a reversal at any index

This is the final, stronger scope. The derivation below is part of the
same joint proof. It does not adopt a previous unverified draft as a
premise. The interpolation and flow arguments are already proved in
Sections2 and6; their hypotheses are checked on the new geometry here.

Choose integers0<=j<K-1<n and a nonnegative word h of length n.
Put P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and assume the global old prefix bounds
h_i<=P_i+2. Let

    P=P_j, r=h_j, b=h_(K-1), H_cut=P+r,
    1<=b<=r, beta=1/b,
    h_i<=b for i<j and for j<i<K-1.                (28)

The contracting negative block must itself satisfy the old prefix
bounds, when measured from its own beginning:

    R_i=sum_(j+1<=t<i)h_t,
    h_i<=R_i+2 for j+1<=i<K.                       (29)

If b<=2, ALL of(29) follows automatically from(28), because every
step in that block is at most b. In this important whole subfamily the
only energy sign assumptions beyond the chronology are the original
global prefix-step bounds. If b>2, condition(29) is an explicit extra
restriction on the family; it is not claimed necessary for the full
original cone.

Use any d>=1, u_q,v>=0 and slow curvatures0<alpha_q<beta with
alpha_q*h_i<=1 for every i. Define p_(i,q) as in(2), and replace z_i by

    z_i=product_(t<i)(1-beta*h_t) for0<=i<=K,
    z_i=0 for i>K.                                (30)

Then z_0>=...>=z_j>=0, z_(j+1)=(1-r/b)*z_j<=0,
z_(j+1)<=z_(j+2)<=...<=z_K=0. The same diagonal quadratic defines
the base geometry x_i=(u_q*p_(i,q),v*z_i) and
g_i=(alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q),beta*v*z_i). Genuine single reversal and
delayed annihilation mean v>0, z_j>0, r>b, and h_i<b for j<i<K-1.
All boundaries are allowed without asserting that the reversal remains
strict. The number of negative samples K-j-1 is arbitrary.

Keep U_q and B from Section1. For j+1<=k<=K put

    A_k=sum_q U_q*[(p_(j,q)-p_(k,q))^2
                         -sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Delta_(l,q)^2],
    B_k=B*[sum_(l=k)^(K-1)(z_l-z_(l+1))^2
                                   +(z_j-z_k)^2-z_j^2],
    delta_cut=min_(j+1<=k<=K)(A_k+B_k),
    X_all=2H_cut*A_K.                             (31)

All A_k and B_k are nonnegative. For A_k expand a square of a sum of
nonnegative slow gaps. For B_k expand (z_j-z_k)^2-z_j^2
=z_k^2-2z_j*z_k>=0, because z_j>=0 and z_k<=0. Also B_K=0.

Define L_slow by the same all-index formula(4). For the positive fast
prefix let a_i^+=beta*v*z_i for i<=j and t_i^+=a_i^+-a_(i+1)^+ for i<j.
For the negative fast block let a_i^-=-beta*v*z_i for j+1<=i<=K and
t_i^-=a_i^--a_(i+1)^- for j+1<=i<K. Put

    E_pos=sum_(i<j)P_(i+1)*t_i^+
                     *[2(1+P_i)*a_i^+-P_(i+1)*t_i^+],
    E_neg=sum_(j+1<=i<K)R_(i+1)*t_i^-
                     *[2(1+R_i)*a_i^--R_(i+1)*t_i^-],
    H_general=E_pos+H_cut*(P+2-r)*(a_j^+)^2+E_neg. (32)

The EXACT minimum over ALL directed finite-compatible values is

    V_min=L_slow+H_general-2H_cut*delta_cut.        (33)

Requiring the actual optimum jet0 does not change this minimum. Its
attaining static function and full minimizer set are(25)--(27), using
the distances proved next. The COMPLETE comparison is

    L_slow>=X_all+H_cut*r*(P+2-r)
                              *sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2*p_(j,q)^2,
    0<=2H_cut*delta_cut<=X_all,
    V_min>=H_general>=0.                          (34)

Thus every finite delay is redundant throughout(28)--(29). In
particular, for b<=2, adding arbitrary monotone slow modes and
optimizing ALL legal joint values gives no new primitive obstruction
after a single reversal at any index and any later annihilation.

### 9.1 Exact original-flow price and every directed constraint

Separate slow distances are still(11). The separate fast distances are

    d_i^fast=B*[z_j^2+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)(z_l-z_(l+1))^2] for i<=j,
    d_i^fast=B*sum_(l=i)^(K-1)(z_l-z_(l+1))^2 for j<i<K,
    d_i^fast=0 for i>=K.                           (35)

The ordered-gap argument proves these formulas on the positive and
negative sides, respectively. Excursions to the opposite side cannot
decrease scalar squared-distance path cost. Let d_i^sep be the sum of
(35) and all separate slow distances. For every i>=j+1 the chronological
path n,...,i simultaneously attains these minima, hence d_i=d_i^sep.

For i<=j propose

    d_i=d_i^sep+delta_cut.                         (36)

The paths n,...,k,j,j-1,...,i have costs d_i^sep+A_k+B_k, so at least
one such path attains(36). Every directed distance inequality is now
checked explicitly to prove that no other path is cheaper.

The sum d^sep of scalar distance potentials satisfies every such
inequality for the summed costs. Within the early group i<=j or within
the later group i>=j+1, the added constant delta_cut cancels. For an
edge whose source is early and target is later, it only weakens the
inequality. Consider a later source k and an early target i. In every
slow coordinate and in the fast coordinate, the position of k is at
most that of j; the points j,j-1,...,i are coordinatewise nondecreasing.
Expanding nonnegative coordinate gaps therefore gives

    w_(k,i)>=w_(k,j)+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)w_(l+1,l),
    d_i^sep=d_j^sep+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)w_(l+1,l).

It suffices to check the target j. If j<k<=K, its slack relative to
d^sep is exactly A_k+B_k>=delta_cut. If k>=K, all fast coordinates
there are zero; insert slow ordered points k-1,...,K into k->j.
The squared-gap inequality gives

    d_k^sep+w_(k,j)>=d_K^sep+w_(K,j)
                                      =d_j^sep+A_K>=d_j^sep+delta_cut.

This proves the remaining directed inequalities. The proposed d_n=0
and these inequalities imply the corresponding lower bound on every
path from n, while the stated paths attain it. Thus(36) is exact.

The common extra distance on all early sinks costs
2sum_(i<=j)h_i*delta_cut=2H_cut*delta_cut. Sending each early sink
along its minimizing crossing path and every later sink chronologically
attains that price with the ORIGINAL terminal-source flow. This proves
the price part of(33), with all pairs retained. It proves no PSD claim
about the resulting auxiliary flow.

The fast terminal is zero and every slow point is at least its terminal
point. Thus the entire fixed-optimum argument(24)--(27) applies:
the optimum does not shorten finite distances; the simultaneous values
are legal for all ordered pairs; one static F attains them; K_min and
R_true in(27) are the actual full minimizer set and actual radius.

### 9.2 Separate fast energy and its sign

For the scalar fast values, the smallest positive sampled position is
z_j, so its optimal value is (a_j^+)^2/2. The positive-prefix value
differences attain the ordered equalities used to derive(16). The
negative side realizes the reflected monotone block with exact energy
E_neg and terminal value and gradient zero. No positive-prefix position
is inserted into its negative ordered path.

Split the original scalar primitive energy into the prefix up to j,
the step r at j, and the negative block. The prefix's own energy is
E_pos and subtracts P(P+1)*(a_j^+)^2. Restoring that term and shifting
its value reference from f_j to the zero final value adds
[P(P+1)+P]*(a_j^+)^2. The step r contributes r*(2-r)*(a_j^+)^2.
Their total added coefficient is

    P(P+1)+P+r*(2-r)
                       =(P+r)*(P+2-r)=H_cut*(P+2-r).

This proves the EXACT expression H_general in(32). Its terms are
nonnegative by the global prefix bounds, the bound r<=P+2, and the
local negative-block bounds(29), respectively, using inequality(17)
with the appropriate local partial sums. Together with the exact
price, this proves(33).

### 9.3 A direct complete budget for an arbitrarily long bypass

Fix one slow coordinate alpha,u. Its optimized energy is(16).
All terms at indices i>=K are nonnegative by the global prefix bounds,
so it suffices to use the first K terms. Their sum is the exact scalar
primitive energy for the prefix word of length K, by the same
telescoping derivation of(16).

If u*p_j=0, this coordinate's A_K is zero and all its energy terms
are nonnegative; the desired comparison follows. Otherwise put

    a_j=alpha*u*p_j, q=p_K/p_j,
    T_neg=sum_(j+1<=i<K)h_i,
    ell=1-r*alpha,
    q_bar=ell/(1+alpha*T_neg).                     (37)

All quantities here are defined for this coordinate. Monotonicity and
the GD products imply0<=q<=q_bar<=1. For completeness the elementary
product bound is

    product_(j+1<=i<K)(1-alpha*h_i)
                                 <=1/(1+alpha*T_neg).

To prove it inductively, if A is the sum of previous nonnegative
factors alpha*h_i and t is the next factor in[0,1], then
(1-t)*(1+A+t)=1+A-t*A-t^2<=1+A. Multiplying the previous product
by this inequality proves the bound, including factors equal to1.

Let E_pre,alpha be the slow energy of the prefix ending at j, and let
E_tail,alpha be its slow energy on the word(h_(j+1),...,h_(K-1)) with
local partial sums beginning at zero. By(17),

    E_pre,alpha>=0, E_tail,alpha>=0,               (38)

using respectively the global prefix bounds and(29).

Subtracting this coordinate's full bypass charge2H_cut*A_K,alpha
from the first K slow terms has an exact algebraic description.
Decrease every value at indices i<=j by A_K,alpha and leave all later
values unchanged. The change in V is exactly-2H_cut*A_K,alpha.
The new value difference between j and K is the direct-edge value

    f_j'-f_K=alpha*u^2*(p_j^2-p_K^2)/2
                          -U*(p_j-p_K)^2,

or equivalently

    2(f_j'-f_K)/a_j^2
           =1+2(1/alpha-1)*q-(2/alpha-1)*q^2.

These temporary comparison values need not be interpolable; this step
is ONLY an algebraic bound on the cost of the explicit bypass path.
Actual attaining values have already been proved legal in9.1.

Split the energy of these comparison values at j and j+1. Restoring
the prefix terminal term contributes P(P+1)*a_j^2, the step r
contributes r*(1-r)*a_j^2, and the shift in value references contributes
2H_cut*(f_j'-f_K). The final gradient coefficient differs from that
of E_tail,alpha by-H_cut*(H_cut+2T_neg+1)*a_j^2*q^2. Combining them gives

    first_K_slow_terms-2H_cut*A_K,alpha
      =E_pre,alpha+E_tail,alpha+H_cut*a_j^2*J(q),
    J(q)=P+2-r+2q*[(1/alpha-1)
                              -(1/alpha+H_cut/2+T_neg)*q]. (39)

This also checks the prefix weights explicitly, instead of treating
the reversal as a new initial point without paying its earlier mass.

The function J is concave on the interval[0,q_bar], so it is at least
the smaller of its two endpoint values. The first is

    J(0)=P+2-r>=0.

For the second, use q_bar=(1-r*alpha)/(1+alpha*T_neg) to obtain

    J(q_bar)=P+2-r+2(r-1)*q_bar-H_cut*q_bar^2
            =(1-q_bar)*[P+2-r+H_cut*q_bar]>=0.     (40)

The equality uses H_cut-(P+2-r)=2(r-1). All its factors have the
required signs by the GLOBAL old prefix bound at the reversal.
There is a useful quantitative margin. Since q_bar<=1-r*alpha and
0<=r*alpha<=1, BOTH endpoints in(40) and J(0) are at least
r*alpha*(P+2-r). Concavity therefore gives

    J(q)>=r*alpha*(P+2-r).

Equations(38)--(40) prove

    L_alpha>=2H_cut*A_K,alpha
                   +H_cut*r*(P+2-r)*alpha^3*u^2*p_j^2.

This holds for EVERY permitted slow curvature. Summing over modes
proves the quantitative first inequality in(34). Since B_K=0, the
k=K path in(31) gives2H_cut*delta_cut<=X_all, proving ALL of(34).
Combining the same margin with(32) also gives the explicit bound

    V_min>=E_pos+E_neg+H_cut*(P+2-r)*
                 [(beta*v*z_j)^2+r*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2*p_(j,q)^2]. (41)

For j=0 this recovers the strict initial bound(6) for EVERY finite
delay, without extending the degree-five polynomial calculation.

No number of intervening negative steps, amplitude optimization,
nonseparable choice of values, or choice among the competing crossing
paths can defeat this bound within(28)--(29). It is a complete
redundancy theorem for the entire newly stated family.

### 9.4 Final scope and the original four-step interface

The result includes every initial single reversal followed by an
arbitrarily delayed annihilation: then r<=2 by the global bound, so
b<=r<=2 and(29) is automatic. It also includes a reversal at ANY later
index with killing step b<=2, regardless of the size of r permitted
by r<=P_j+2. It includes further b>2 cases whenever the explicit
standalone negative-block bounds(29) hold.

For n=4, all possible reversal/killing locations satisfy0<=j<K-1<4.
In particular the previously open delayed middle pattern
h=(a,r,c,b), j=1, K=4 is covered whenever a<=b<=2, b<=r<=a+2,
0<=c<=b, together with the already stated global bounds. The genuinely
delayed interior has a<b, r>b and0<c<b. No capacity is held fixed:
C=1+a+r+c+b can lie in the high-capacity range C>=193/25.

Zero steps at any position, repeated fast or full points, earlier
annihilation on a boundary, vanished amplitudes, zero products and
unit curvature remain in the proof. Every ordered pair and all original
formal gradient coordinates are retained; no quotient of the ambient
R^5 certificate space has been taken. The original h/Lambda/exact-flow/
full-PSD problem of04219554e41d9750 is preserved.

The exact gain2H_cut*delta_cut can be positive, as the explicit
three-path interior of Section1 demonstrates. The budget(39)--(40)
shows why it supplies no new exclusion here. Modes nonzero at the
terminal, multiple reversals or multiple changing modes, and suffixes
failing(29) remain outside the final theorem. General full-cone seed
feasibility and the original asymptotic exact-profile target stay open.

All statements in this extension remain UNVERIFIED joint drafts.
No fact has been submitted or represented as verifier-accepted.
