# Exact survivor of the complete current scalar necessary-test system

Status: self-contained joint proof DRAFT, not a verifier-accepted fact.
This file establishes a limitation of a specified collection of necessary
conditions. It does NOT supply nonnegative multipliers or a full PSD seed.
No optimizer, numerical parameter search, grid, verifier, or installation
was used. The two tiny exact rational calculations expand the fixed
polynomial identities printed below; they do not search for a schedule.

## 1. Exact schedule and variable-capacity family

For 0<=t<=1/100 define

 h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4),
 a=h0-1=2/5, b=h1-1=5/4, c=h2-1=8/25+t, d=h3-1=3/4,
 C=1+sum h=193/25+t,
 P=2+a=12/5, Q=3+a+b=93/20,
 D=2+d=11/4, H=3+c+d=407/100+t.

All previous max2 scalar tests, and the complete C82 triangle family,
are STRICTLY satisfied throughout 0<=t<=1/100. In addition, every
quadratic-curvature test and every C83 cut-repair test is STRICTLY
satisfied for every 0<k<=1 throughout 0<=t<=10^(-12).

Thus there is an exact survivor at total sum168/25 and a concrete
interval of survivors with strictly larger sums. In particular an
attempt to prove emptiness from these scalar tests cannot succeed.
This is not a restriction or a reduction of the original feasible set
onto a fixed-capacity slice; it is a constructed family inside the
necessary-test region.

The original full interface remains twenty Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j in0..4,
Lambda_ii=0, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,
L^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2sum h)^T, and

 diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)) + (LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0,
 s=sum h.

No Lambda or matrix remainder is claimed in this file. Accepted
04219554e41d9750 gives the conditional asymptotic implication only
if an actual full certificate is subsequently constructed.

## 2. Compact box, every cut, and the elementary nonlinear tests

On our interval, 193/25<=C<=773/100<87/10, 8/25<=c<=33/100,
and all four shifts are positive. The endpoint bounds a,d<77/97 and
middle bounds b,c<1552559/1081453 hold: b=5/4 gives
4*1552559-5*1081453=802971>0, and c< b. Thus the strict step box
from the earlier joint draft is satisfied.

The four cut inequalities, in order, are

 Ca <= C-1-a,
 Cb <= P H,
 Cc <= Q D,
 Cd <= C-1-d.                                           (1)

The first and fourth have margins respectively
(3/5)C-7/5>0 and C/4-7/4>0. The second has margin

 PH-Cb=59/500+(23/20)t>0.                                (2)

For the third, Cc<=25509/10000<64/25, whereas QD=1023/80>12.
These bounds follow by multiplying the upper endpoints of C and c.
Every prefix/reversed-prefix cut recurrence is merely a rearrangement
of (1), so no extra cut has been dropped. The concavity-derived endpoint
sum condition follows from the two middle cuts: if E=4+a+d, adding
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P), c<=D(C-D)/(C+D), and using the negative second
derivative of z(C-z)/(C+z), gives E>=2C(C-1)/(3C+1).
The pointwise endpoint and middle cut bounds follow directly from (1).

The actual unit-curvature product test, suffix product test, and
chronological clipped-tail test are, respectively,

 C a b c d<=1,   C c d<=Q,
 C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P.                              (3)

Here ab=1/2, bc<=33/80<1, and Cc<64/25. Consequently their left
sides are less than

 (3/8)(64/25)=24/25<1,
 (3/4)(64/25)=48/25<Q,
 (15/16)(64/25)=12/5=P.

All comparisons are strict because 25509<25600.
At t=0 the exact full product is579/625 and the exact clipped
left side is579/250, whose gap from P is21/250.

The scalar mixed first-prefix test has u=7/5 and eta=b/H. Its expression
is

 u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-a C^2 eta^2
   =59/25+(7/125)eta-(2/5)(C eta)^2.

By (1), C eta<P=12/5. Therefore this expression exceeds7/125>0.
Its domain a eta<1 also follows since a eta<ab/4=1/8.

For the two unit-curvature cut repairs, the base energy is
V0=(1+prefix sum)^2-C^2 eta^2>0 by (1). The auxiliary terminal
value q is q=1/(2H) at cut1, and q=-c/(2D) at cut2. Hence

 C|q|=C/(2H)<1 at cut1, since 2H-C=C-73/10>0,
 C|q|=Cc/(2D)<(64/25)/(11/2)<1 at cut2.

The auxiliary energy1-C^2 q^2 is strictly positive. Thus every repair
expression beta V0+eta(1+eta)(1-C^2 q^2) is positive for beta>=0,
including the beta=0 case. No denominator with an unproved sign is used.

## 3. All three adjacent triangles and the first backward lift

The adjacent-triangle tests, with the selected consecutive shifts
B,E, preceding capacity P0 and following capacity D0, read

 B E [1+B(P0+C)/P0^2] <= D0.                            (4)

For the first pair, its left side is
ab[1+a(1+C)] < (1/2)[1+(2/5)*9]=23/10<H.
For the middle pair, bc<=33/80, P+C<11, and P^2>5. Therefore its
left side is less than

 (33/80)[1+(5/4)*11/5]=99/64<11/4=D.

For the last pair, cd<=99/400, Q+C<13 and Q^2>21. Its left side
is less than

 (99/400)[1+(33/100)*13/21]
 <(99/400)(5/4)=99/320<1.

The strengthened middle triangle has the additional nonnegative
parameter max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0). In this family

 a^2+2a-1=-1/25,

so this parameter equals zero and the strengthened test is exactly
the already strict middle inequality. This is an actual branch check,
not deletion of an active max term.

## 4. Both last-pair lifts and the earliest-normal guard

Define the last-triangle quantities

 eta=c/D,
 K=c^2 d+(c+d+2)(1-cd)=c+d+2-cd(d+2),
 r=c^2 d/K,
 Hpre=2+a+b=73/20, Apre=1+a+b=53/20,
 Z=(c+r)/(1+d),
 qlate=(1+(1-b)eta)/b,
 qearly=[eta+(Apre eta-1)Z]/Apre,
 Jpre=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 max(qlate,qearly).

The required inequalities are C^2 r<=min(Jpre,Q^2) and

 C^2 max(eta^2,r)<=Jquad,
 Jquad=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).             (5)

All denominators are positive: eta<3/25, cd<1/4, c+d+2>3, so
K>3-(1/4)(11/4)=37/16>2. Also

 r < ((33/100)^2*(3/4))/2 <1/20,
 eta^2<9/625<1/20, C^2<64.

Thus every left side in (5) and C^2 r is less than16/5.
On the right, qlate>(1-3/100)/(5/4)=97/125>3/4,
Hpre>7/2,Hpre<4, and eta<3/25. Therefore

 Jpre >=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 qlate
       >1-24/25+147/16>9.

Also Q^2>21. For Jquad use b eta<3/20, a^2 b=1/5 and1+b=9/4:

 Jquad >[(17/20)*21+5]/(9/4)=457/45>10.

These bounds already retain the full max in Jpre. In fact the earliest
normal is inactive here for a proved reason: Apre eta<(53/20)(3/25)<1.
Since Z>0, the explicit formula gives
qearly<eta/Apre<eta<3/25<3/4<qlate. No shortcut based only on
cd<1 or b eta<1 has been made.
The other max is also correctly determined: d=3/4>sqrt(2)-1, so
r>eta^2. This follows from

d(d+2)^2-K=(c+d+2)(d(d+2)-1)>0.

At t=0, K=241/100,r=192/6025,eta=32/275,
qlate=1068/1375, and Jquad=529303/49500.

## 5. The entire C82 interior-edge triangle family survives

For clarity, the interface is rederived here in this family's notation.
Take U>0,z>0, with

 U>=b,
 z^2 <= (U+1)(a U-1),
 z^2 >= c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2.                              (6)

Use vertices p=(U,0), q=(-1,z), w=(-bc/D,0), and the convex
function F(x)=max_{v in conv(p,q,w)}(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).
Its gradient is projection onto that triangle. The original jets are

 x0=((1+a)U-1,z), g0=(U,0),
 x1=(-1,z), g1=(-1,z),
 x2=(b,-bz), g2=(b,0),
 x3=(-bc,-bz), g3=(-bc/D,0),
 x4=(-bc/D,-bz), g4=(-bc/D,0).

The initial normal comparison with q is z^2-(U+1)(aU-1)<=0;
the other initial comparison is nonpositive since aU-1>=0.
At1 the normal is0. At2 its normal is(0,-bz), and U>=b places
g2 on the base. At3 the comparison with q is
b[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0, and the base comparison is nonpositive.
At4 the normal is again vertical downward. These checks include every
triangle vertex and the optimum0 in its base, hence all original
interpolation pairs. The four GD identities follow by subtraction.
Saturated values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 telescope, giving energy

 E(U,z)=((1+a)U-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2(bc/D)^2.           (7)

The complete parameter-eliminated C82 test is the infimum of (7)
over (6), including zero-height limits. We bound the whole domain,
so no unproved formula for that infimum is needed.
Since a=2/5, (6) forces U>=5/2. Also 1-b^2=-9/16<0, so
E(U,z)>=q(U), where

 q(U)=((7/5)U-1)^2-(9/16)(U+1)((2/5)U-1)-C^2(bc/D)^2.

Its quadratic coefficient is347/200>0 and
q'(5/2)=(7/5)(71/16)>0. Thus q is strictly increasing for U>=5/2.
Using Cc<64/25 and b/D=5/11 gives

 E(U,z)>=q(5/2)=25/4-(5 Cc/11)^2
              >25/4-25/16=75/16>0.

This includes all branches, boundary limits and the exact infimum.
It proves survival of C82, not sufficiency of any scalar test.

## 6. All curvatures in the full quadratic and C83 families

This section first fixes t=0. For 0<k<=1 and j in{0,1,2,3}, put

 R_j=sum_(i>j)h_i, H_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i, E_j=1+k R_j,
 p_0=1, p_i=product_(v<i)(1-k h_v),
 q_j=k p_(j+1)/E_j.

The auxiliary trajectory follows the curvature-k quadratic through
query j, and subsequently has constant gradient q_j, ending at
x4=q_j/k. Its values before or at j are k p_i^2/2 and afterwards
q_j^2/(2k)+q_j^2 sum_(v>=i)h_v. This array is only an auxiliary
array; it need not be legal before a repair. Direct substitution in

 V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2
   -s(s+1)g4^2

gives

 V_j(k)=k sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2
        -H_j[1/k+s+R_j+1]q_j^2
       =k P_j(k)/E_j^2,
 P_j=E_j^2 sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2
       -H_j[1+k(s+R_j+1)]p_(j+1)^2.                   (8)

For the substitution, the suffix values give
2sum_(i>j)h_i sum_(v>=i)h_v=R_j^2+sum_(i>j)h_i^2;
combining with the suffix gradient diagonals leaves R_j(R_j+1)q_j^2.
Subtracting s(s+1)q_j^2 and the prefix terminal values yields (8).
In particular j=3 is the actual quadratic F(x)=k x^2/2 for all queries.

The constant and linear coefficients of P_j vanish for every schedule.
The constant is H_j-H_j. Write T_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i sum_(v<i)h_v;
then 2T_j=H_j^2-sum_(i<=j)h_i^2. The coefficient of k in the prefix
sum is H_j-H_j^2. Hence the coefficient of k in P_j is
2R_j H_j+H_j-H_j^2-H_j(s+R_j+1-2H_j)=0,
since s=H_j+R_j. Thus P_j=k^2 Q_j, where Q_j is a polynomial.

For j=0, Q_0 is linear and its endpoint values are

 Q_0(0)=256368/3125, Q_0(1)=475104/15625,

both positive. For j=1,2,3, all coefficients in the exact Bernstein
expansions on [0,1/2] and [1/2,1] are positive, as printed below.
Since the Bernstein basis polynomials are nonnegative and sum to1,
Q_j(k)>0 throughout [0,1], and V_j(k)>0 throughout 0<k<=1.

For the C83 repair at a selected cut j=0,1,2, its base cut energy is
V0_j=(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2-C^2 eta_j^2>0, eta_j=(h_j-1)/(1+R_j).
The entire curvature test has form

 beta_j(k) V0_j + eta_j(1+eta_j) V_j(k)>=0,

where beta_j(k) is the maximum of0 and the finite residual-repair
ratios. In particular beta_j(k)>=0, including all changing maximizer
branches and beta=0. Both energies have just been proved positive,
so the expression is strictly positive for every curvature and every
branch. No active-residual formula or denominator sign is discarded.
For j=3 the auxiliary is already the full legal quadratic and its
positive energy has been checked directly. The curvature-zero limit
is obtained by continuity; no division by k=0 is asserted.

## 7. Exact polynomial identity certificate

For a degree m polynomial Q(k)=sum_i A_i k^i, its Bernstein coefficients
on [0,1] are B_v=sum_(i<=v) A_i binom(v,i)/binom(m,i).
This identity follows by expanding k^i in the degree-m Bernstein basis.
To obtain the halves, repeatedly replace consecutive coefficients by
their average. The first entries down this triangular array give the
coefficients on [0,1/2], and the last entries in reverse row order give
those on [1/2,1]. This follows from splitting each degree-m Bernstein
basis at its midpoint, or directly from the binomial theorem.

The following exact integer arrays specify Q and the two expansions.
Each array is divided by the stated positive denominator. These are
small, fixed polynomial identities, checked with rational arithmetic;
no sample of k or optimization result is used as a premise.

j=1; power denominator=250000; power numerators=[50793540, -177528981, 237836151, -110684385]
left: denominator=2000000; numerators=[406348320, 169643012, 91495138, 61220313]; all positive=True
right: denominator=2000000; numerators=[61220313, 30945488, 48543712, 3330600]; all positive=True
j=2; power denominator=2500000000; power numerators=[633255870000, -3594456911400, 8982042049995, -11659202708349, 7719844643973, -2066390991819]
left: denominator=400000000000; numerators=[101320939200000, 43809628617600, 22226486235180, 13253106636042, 9010773691434, 6728505601455]; all positive=True
right: denominator=400000000000; numerators=[6728505601455, 4446237511476, 4124034276126, 2990914095504, 3383629498560, 2414712384000]; all positive=True
j=3; power denominator=2500000000; power numerators=[738069932500, -6435214284525, 25550374460520, -58327870129494, 81650300956812, -69585624017973, 33277390306776, -6867072767016]
left: denominator=2800000000000; numerators=[826638324400000, 311821181638000, 137675698349600, 70890394016824, 41454390035320, 26681369684405, 18427013864050, 13413025887415]; all positive=True
right: denominator=2800000000000; numerators=[13413025887415, 8399037910780, 6625417777865, 4813868801830, 4552116709024, 2617934431520, 4409755955200, 396992512000]; all positive=True

## 8. A concrete interval above the threshold also survives all curvatures

Every Bernstein coefficient in Section7 and each endpoint of Q_0
exceeds1/10. This is an exact comparison of the displayed integers.
We retain the same degree for Q_j under variation of t.

For a polynomial A(k), let ||A||_1 be the sum of the absolute values
of its coefficients. This norm is submultiplicative by convolution.
Throughout 0<=t<=1/100, all h_i<3, s<7,R_j<7,H_j<7.
Only h2 depends on t, with derivative1. Thus for i<=4,

 ||p_i||_1<=256, ||partial_t p_i||_1<=64,
 ||p_i^2||_1<=65536, ||partial_t(p_i^2)||_1<=32768.

In the prefix sum Z_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2,
i<=3, so ||p_i^2||_1<=4096. The linear coefficient polynomial
has norm h_i^2<9, and its derivative has norm at most6. Therefore

 ||Z_j||_1<=147456,
 ||partial_t Z_j||_1<=4(6*4096+9*32768)=1277952.

Also ||E_j||_1<=8, ||partial_t E_j||_1<=1,
||1+k(s+R_j+1)||_1<=16, and its derivative norm is at most2.
Differentiating (8), and using H_j<=7 and partial_t H_j<=1, yields

 ||partial_t P_j||_1
 <=16*147456+64*1277952+30*65536+112*32768
 =89784320 <10^8.

Division by k^2 just deletes the two identically zero coefficients,
so the same bound holds for partial_t Q_j. Each Bernstein coefficient
has derivative magnitude bounded by this norm, because
0<=binom(v,i)/binom(m,i)<=1; midpoint subdivision takes convex
combinations and preserves the bound. Consequently for
0<=t<=10^(-12), every displayed positive coefficient changes by
less than10^(-4). It remains greater than1/10-1/10000>0.
This proves the all-curvature assertions on that exact interval.

Sections2--5 already proved every other scalar test throughout the
larger interval 0<=t<=1/100. Thus, for example, the exact rational word

 (7/5,9/4,33/25+10^(-12),7/4)

has sum168/25+10^(-12) and passes the ENTIRE specified scalar system,
including the continuum of curvatures and all C82 parameters.

## 9. Exact failed implication and what remains open

The failed implication is:

 'All the cut, quadratic, clipped-tail, adjacent-triangle, backward-lift,
 repaired-last-triangle, C82 and C83 inequalities together exclude every
 four-step schedule with sum at least168/25.'

The displayed rational family disproves that implication. It does not
prove existence of full Lambda/M certificates, and no converse from
these witnesses is supplied. Even an explicit full-cone obstruction for
this family would not eliminate all other schedules.

All cut slacks are genuinely positive. At t=0 the critical second cut
has slack59/500 and the last cut has slack9/50. Neither generic
cut saturation nor an automatic tail restriction is a legal inference.
The remaining exact constructive question is the original full
20-multiplier/five-gradient feasibility problem for an explicit word;
the remaining global exclusion question needs a witness or coefficient
argument beyond the now-refuted scalar coverage claim.

# One exposed triangle: coupled side transport, failed capture, and a paid full-word face switch

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No new fact ID. Original minimax target OPEN.**

Worker max4, project gd-exact-profile-20260926, invocation88 after ended87.
Guidance5c716c2407472849; plan229ca5c7b9b482af. Exactly one authorized
invocation, cap1. This report is a joint mathematical draft, not an accepted
fact and not a proof of the full triangle profile. Every new ingredient used
here is proved here. The old scalar report5960de36af8e17d5 is awareness only;
none of its unverified lemmas is an accepted premise of this report.

Exactly TWO substantive mechanisms were opened. Mechanism1 derives the
actual fixed-triangle projection cells, a coupled exact complete-child side
identity, and legal failures of the scalar tube/capture migration. Only
after those decisive failures, Mechanism2 treats ONE face-switch pattern:
the first ORIGINAL leaf changes upper side to lower side, and every remaining
original sample, including the terminal, is on that lower side. Its full
parameter domain is paid analytically, uniformly over the angle and cap.
An explicit finite off-axis full-word member proves the pattern is legal.

No mathematical programs, arithmetic/numerical/trajectory/recurrence replays,
solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls/fact_submit, installations,
consults, subagents, external publication, controller/service actions,
finalization, revocation or Goal completion were performed. Tool uses were
administrative text reads/writes and shared-memory/literature retrieval.

## 1. Exact scope and outcome

For all the parameters below, the candidate conclusions established jointly
in this report are:

1. Every sloping-side complete child, with all its original queries AND its
   terminal in that same side cell, obeys the sharp coupled identity(12).
   Its planar objective score is at most1/(1+2S_j), with the SAME enclosing
   m in every child. Height and the normal component are never optimized
   independently.
2. The scalar interval-distance tube does not extend to the triangle even
   through a legal complete H_1. Section6 gives exact finite parameters and
   both actual projection guards.
3. The scalar small-radius terminal capture fails for an actual complete
   H_m, including a finite orthogonal T=1 lift with TRUE initial radius
   less than d_m. Its score is still below1/D. Thus this is a failure of
   an intermediate invariant, not a counterexample to the target bound.
4. Every legal member of the single first-leaf upper-to-lower switch pattern
   in Section8 has planar and finite-tilt score STRICTLY below1/D. This is
   uniform in the triangle angle, size, start and all m>=1536. It is a
   closed all-depth pattern result, not a cell list or a finite experiment.

The unrestricted all-start all-L,H triangle bound(1+C/m)/D with an absolute C
has NOT been proved. No all-depth strict witness above17/(16D) was obtained.
Repeated switches, changes involving the base or its endpoints, and
unrestricted returns remain outside the proved pattern. No full-W bound or
resolution of PROBLEM.md follows. Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 requires a
FULL-class upper bound; accepted factd8b59c7d92425121 does not reduce its
arbitrary polytopes to these triangles.

## 2. Word, geometry, objective and notation

Fix ONE integer m>=1536. Define

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, D_j=1+2S_j, S=S_m, D=D_m, n=2^m-1,
    ell=1+a, p_j=product_(h in H_j)(h-1), p_0=1, Q=p_m^2.

The physical entries h_i, 0<=i<n, are exactly H_m in their original order.
All prefixes below are original prefixes; no occurrence is omitted. The
quantity d_m is auxiliary, not an additional physical step. The symbol H_j
for a word is distinct from the positive triangle height H.

Let L,H>0 be finite. In the plane define

    K=conv{0,(L,H),(L,-H)}, alpha=(1,0),
    phi(z)=min_(y in K)[alpha.y+||z-y||^2/2],
    y(z)=P_K(z-alpha), g(z)=z-y(z),
    F_h(z)=z-h*g(z),
    Psi_j=the chronological composition along H_j.

P_K denotes Euclidean projection. The original function has advertised
smoothness1. For any advertised smoothness M_sm>0, use f=M_sm*phi and
steps h_i/M_sm; the normalized scores are unchanged and remain W=2R.

Introduce the finite side length and its direction cosines:

    Rside=sqrt(L^2+H^2), u=L/Rside, v=H/Rside,
    u>0, v>0, u^2+v^2=1.

For a sign sigma in{+1,-1}, set

    e_sigma=(u,sigma*v), n_sigma=(-v,sigma*u),
    r_sigma=e_sigma.z, w_sigma=n_sigma.z.

Each pair is orthonormal; n_sigma is the outward normal of that sloping
side. In Section8 only, kappa=u^2-v^2 and omega=2u*v denote the relative
rotation coefficients, so kappa^2+omega^2=1. The triangle has no other
vertices, coordinates or freely selectable face data.

The elementary projection characterization is

    y=P_K(z-alpha) iff
    (z-alpha-y).(b-y)<=0 for every b in K.             (1)

It suffices to test the three vertices, by linearity in b. Necessity comes
from differentiating squared distance along segments from y; sufficiency
comes from expanding ||z-alpha-b||^2. Completing the square in phi shows
that this projection is its unique minimizing y and its gradient is g.
The convexity, differentiability,1-smoothness and unique minimum0 can also
be read from accepted factd8b59c7d92425121, applied to this particular K
and exposing alpha. The exposing condition holds because every nonzero
point of K has strictly positive first coordinate. These conclusions do
not make the new trajectory estimates accepted facts.

## 3. All actual projection cells, including ties

Write z=(s,t). The following CLOSED cells cover the plane; overlap on
boundaries gives the same y,g and objective. The formulas follow by(1).

Origin vertex:

    u*(s-1)+v*|t|<=0;
    y=0, g=z, 2phi=||z||^2.                          (2)

Interior of the triangle, with its boundary included:

    1<=s<=1+L, |t|<=(H/L)*(s-1);
    y=(s-1,t), g=(1,0), 2phi=2s-1.                 (3)

Sloping side sigma:

    u<=r_sigma<=u+Rside, w_sigma>=-v;
    y=(r_sigma-u)*e_sigma,
    g=u*e_sigma+w_sigma*n_sigma,
    2phi=2u*r_sigma-u^2+w_sigma^2.                  (4)

Indeed z-alpha has coordinates r_sigma-u,w_sigma+v, because alpha has
coordinates u,-v. The first coordinate belongs to the side segment and
the second is a nonnegative outward normal. This proves every normal test
against the ENTIRE fixed triangle. The affine height of this proximal
point is u*(r_sigma-u); it is not discarded.

Vertical base:

    s>=1+L, |t|<=H;
    y=(L,t), g=(s-L,0), 2phi=2L+(s-L)^2.            (5)

Nonzero vertex sigma:

    sigma*t>=H,
    L*(s-1-L)+H*(sigma*t-H)>=0;
    y=(L,sigma*H), g=(s-L,t-sigma*H),
    2phi=2L+(s-L)^2+(t-sigma*H)^2.                 (6)

For(2), the two tests against(L,+/-H) give its inequality. For(3), the
projection residual is zero. For(5), the outward normal is a nonnegative
multiple of(1,0). At the vertex in(6), comparison with the other nonzero
vertex gives sigma*t>=H, and comparison with0 gives the other inequality.
Conversely these tests imply(1). Any projection onto a triangle belongs
to its interior, an edge, or a vertex, so these cases are exhaustive.

For every original query and terminal, define y_i=y(z_i), g_i=z_i-y_i,
f_i=phi(z_i), delta_i=alpha.y_i. Then

    f_i=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2,
    (g_i-alpha).(y_j-y_i)<=0 for ALL i,j.            (7)

All subsequent examples are actual iterates of this one static function.
Equation(7) therefore retains every remote original normal automatically,
rather than selecting compatible-looking faces independently.

For a physical h=1+d, the side law is exactly

    r_sigma'=r_sigma-h*u, w_sigma'=-d*w_sigma.       (8)

On the origin cell z'=-d*z; on the interior cell z'=z-h*alpha; on the
base s'=L-d*(s-L),t'=t; at vertex b_sigma=(L,sigma*H),
z'=b_sigma-d*(z-b_sigma). These formulas are used only under their cells.
The symmetry axis t=0 is the former scalar segment, but all substantive
constructions below have t_0!=0.

## 4. Same-m word estimates, re-proved here

The length is2^j-1. With A_j=1+S_j, the word gives
A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j and A_0=1. Put eta=a/(c-2). Since
c>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c*(c-2)>1>beta, one has0<eta<1. The solution is

    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    A_j>=d_j/(c-2), A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    D_j<2c^j for j>=1.                             (9)

The expression has the right initial value and recurrence, proving it
by induction. The inequalities use c>2 and0<eta<1. Direct elementary
bounds, valid for the stated enclosing m, are

    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2.

For example7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/1000 imply the bounds on c;
those on beta and a follow from their definitions. The product recursion
p_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2 gives

    p_j=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j),
    d_j*p_j=beta^(2^j)/c.                           (10)

The signed quadratic product along each nonempty H_j is -p_j, since its
length is odd. Every nonempty physical prefix of H_j has unsigned product
at most a. Induct: a prefix inside the first child uses the child assertion;
a prefix including the bridge has product p_j*d_j times a right-child
prefix, bounded by1, and p_j*d_j<=a. The base is the sole leaf.

A refinement needed for the switch is that every prefix of length at
least2 has product at most a*beta. The base H_1 has no such prefix. In the
inductive step a prefix in the first child uses induction; a prefix including
the bridge has product at most beta^(2^j)/c<=a*beta for j>=1. This also
covers the bridge immediately after the first leaf in H_2.

Consequently every nonempty complete child satisfies

    D_j*p_j^2<2c^(-j)<1.                            (11)

At the enclosing depth,

    D*Q/a^2<18c^(-m)<1,
    d_m^2/D>c^m/18>4.

The first uses a>1/3 and(9)-(10). The second uses the same estimates.
The final comparisons hold already for m>=7 since c>2. Also S>63 since
H_m contains more than63 positive entries, each exceeding1. All estimates
are uniform in the geometry and start; children keep the original m.

## 5. Mechanism1: a coupled identity through a complete side child

Fix j>=1 and a sloping side sigma. Suppose every original query of a
complete H_j AND its terminal lies in the closed cell(4) of this side.
Write the initial side coordinates r_0,w_0 and the terminal r_end,w_end.
Equation(8), over the actual child, gives

    r_end=r_0-S_j*u, w_end=-p_j*w_0,
    2phi(z_end)=2u*r_0-D_j*u^2+p_j^2*w_0^2.

It follows by expanding one square that

    ||z_0||^2-D_j*2phi(z_end)
      =(r_0-D_j*u)^2+(1-D_j*p_j^2)*w_0^2>=0.       (12)

This couples the transported height to the tangential budget and retains
the complete normal payment. It is not a separate objective envelope and
radius envelope. Equations(8) and(12) compose through both complete H_j
children and their bridge whenever the same-side guards hold throughout:
the tangential loss is the actual sum2S_j+1+d_j and the signed normal
factor is -d_j*p_j^2=-p_(j+1). Thus(12) is an evaluated all-depth invariant
on its guarded domain, with the SAME enclosing parameters.

The planar bound is sharp: take r_0=D_j*u,w_0=0 and Rside>D_j*u. All
prefix tangential coordinates decrease from D_j*u to(S_j+1)*u>=u, every
normal is0>=-v, and all proximal points stay below the finite cap. For the
full word these are off-axis starts for every v>0. Their planar score is
exactly1/D. Their finite-tilt score is paid in Section7.

For comparison, an entire interior-cell child has t constant and s reduced
by S_j; its exact slack is(s_0-D_j)^2+t_0^2. An entire origin-cell child
has score p_j^2<1/D_j. A trajectory cannot stay in the same nonzero vertex
cell across a step h>1: its side-directed residual initially has inner
product at least u>0 with e_sigma, and multiplication by -d makes it
negative. Similarly an entire base-cell trajectory cannot persist across
a step: s-L>=1 is sent to -d*(s-L)<0. These observations do not exclude
returns to those cells later.

### Why the obvious extension is not proved

The coordinates in(12) refer to ONE face. On changing sides, both the
linear height direction and the quadratic normal coordinate rotate.
Using the first child's side identity and then the second child's identity
without paying that rotation changes the original initial-distance term.
No inequality justifying that splice has been obtained for arbitrary
switches. The next two exact constructions show that the former segment
capture arguments cannot supply the missing justification unchanged.

## 6. Two decisive legal failures of scalar tube/capture migration

### 6.1 A complete H_1 violates the interval-distance tube

The scalar tube would suggest, with Delta(z)=dist(z,K),

    Delta(F_(1+d)(z))<=d*max(Delta(z),1).             (FALSE)

Keep the enclosing m and its actual leaf d=a. Set

    u=sqrt(15)/4, v=1/4, Rside=3,
    L=3sqrt(15)/4, H=3/4,
    r_0=u+a/(4sqrt(15)), w_0=2

in upper-side coordinates. The shifted projection is strictly inside the
upper side: r_0-u=a/(4sqrt(15)) is in(0,3), and w_0+v=9/4>0. Its actual
leaf output, by(8), has upper coordinates

    r_1=-7a/(2sqrt(15)), w_1=-2a.

The Euclidean nearest point in K to z_0 is r_0*e_+, since0<r_0<3 and
w_0>0. Thus Delta(z_0)=2. At z_1 the LOWER coordinates are

    r_1^-=11a/(16sqrt(15)), w_1^-=35a/16.

Here0<r_1^-<3 and w_1^->0, so its Euclidean nearest point in K is
r_1^-*e_- and Delta(z_1)=35a/16>2a. The claimed tube fails strictly.

For the objective projection at the terminal, r_1^+<u and r_1^-<u,
so both vertex0 inequalities hold and y(z_1)=0. Thus the initial query is
on the genuine upper-side cell, the actual complete leaf ends in the
origin cell, and both original guards are explicit. There is no freely
chosen output face. In global coordinates the off-axis initial point is

    z_0=((7+a)/16,(135+a)/(16sqrt(15))).

All parameters are finite. Section7 embeds this very same child in three
dimensions with any finite T, without altering its planar dynamics. The
failure concerns the proposed distance tube, not a worst-case objective
bound. In particular retaining only interval distance loses an angular
component even in a complete original child.

### 6.2 A whole H_m defeats the small-radius capture, at finite tilt

Choose the geometry AND start explicitly as follows:

    u=d_m/(2D), v=sqrt(1-u^2),
    Rside=d_m, L=u*d_m, H=v*d_m,
    r_0=d_m/2=D*u, w_0=0, z_0=r_0*e_+.

These are legitimate positive finite triangle parameters. Indeed(9) gives
S>=d_m/(c-2)-1>2d_m-1, hence D>4d_m-1>d_m, since d_m>1.
Thus0<u<1. Every original query and terminal stays on the upper side:

    r_i=u*(D-U_i), w_i=0,
    U_i=sum_(0<=k<i)h_k, 0<=U_i<=S.

Therefore u<=r_i<=d_m/2<u+Rside, w_i>=-v; the terminal has
r_n=u*(S+1)>u. Formula(12) gives

    2phi(z_n)=D*u^2=d_m^2/(4D)>1.                  (13)

The scalar complete-child capture would have put terminal radius below
a<1 and terminal2phi below1 for inputs of radius at most d_m. Both
conclusions fail here. This is an actual entire word, not an incompatible
face prescription.

There is also a finite orthogonal original instance. Set T=1 and take
X_0=(s_0,t_0,-s_0), where(s_0,t_0)=z_0. Then

    ||X_0||^2=r_0^2*(1+u^2)<d_m^2,
    2Phi(X_n)=d_m^2/(4D)+Q*u^2*r_0^2>1.

Its normalized score is exactly

    E=(1/D+u^2*Q)/(1+u^2),
    1/(2D)<E<1/D, and E>1/d_m^2.                 (14)

The first lower bound uses u<1; the last uses d_m^2/D>4. The strict
upper bound uses Q<1/D. Thus the true finite-radius version of the old
small-start score bound also fails, while the requested17/(16D) threshold
is NOT exceeded. Its role is solely to kill that intermediate invariant.

The obstruction is precise: an off-axis ray can have height slope u much
smaller than||alpha||=1. Its tangential drift is h*u, not h. The coupled
side identity retains this u; a radius capture independent of angle loses
it. Neither construction rejects the full triangle research direction.

## 7. The exact finite-tilt embedding and true denominator

Embed the one triangle in the plane with third coordinate0 and put

    Ktilde={(y_1,y_2,0):(y_1,y_2) in K},
    lambda=(1,0,T), T>0,
    Phi(X)=min_(Y in Ktilde)[lambda.Y+||X-Y||^2/2].

For X=(z,q), the definition separates exactly:

    Phi(z,q)=phi(z)+q^2/2, grad Phi=(g(z),q).

The height remains y_1, so0 is still the unique minimum of the full
function. Choose the required orthogonal initial point

    X_0=(s,t,-s/T), lambda.X_0=0.

Then the planar trajectory is exactly the one already derived, and
q_n=(-p_m)*q_0. The TRUE squared initial distance to the complete minimum
set{0} and the exact terminal score are

    Rtrue^2=s^2+t^2+s^2/T^2,
    E_T=[2phi(z_n)+Q*s^2/T^2]/[s^2+t^2+s^2/T^2].     (15)

For z_0!=0, define E_plane=2phi(z_n)/(s^2+t^2) and
chi=s^2/[T^2*(s^2+t^2)]. Then

    E_T=(E_plane+chi*Q)/(1+chi).                    (16)

This is an exact convex combination, not a free height correction. Any
planar upper at least Q passes to finite tilt. If E_plane=1/D and s!=0,
every finite T has E_T<1/D. The supremum1/D in the fixed-side subfamily is
approached as finite T grows, not attained by declaring T=infinity.
The instances in Sections6 and10 choose an actual finite T. A hypothetical
strict planar witness above a target q_target>Q would require
T^2>s^2*(q_target-Q)/[(s^2+t^2)*(E_plane-q_target)] for its finite lift to
remain strict; no such witness is claimed here.

## 8. Mechanism2: exactly one first-leaf upper-to-lower switch pattern

This mechanism starts only after the decisive failures in Section6. It
opens just the following pattern, for the ORIGINAL H_m:

    original query0: upper sloping-side cell;
    original samples1,2,...,n: lower sloping-side cell.

The terminal condition is part of the pattern. Boundary ties are allowed
in the general bound; Section10 supplies a member with strict side guards.
The entire remainder of H_m stays present. This does not authorize a
second pattern at the root or independent face choices within a child.

Let(r,w) be the lower coordinates of the actual state z_1 after the first
leaf ell. Write

    M=S-ell, Drem=1+2M=D-2ell,
    kappa=u^2-v^2, omega=2u*v.

The change of coordinates is

    e_-.e_+=kappa, e_-.n_+=-omega,
    n_-.e_+=-omega, n_-.n_+=-kappa.

Inverting just the first physical leaf via(8) gives its UNIQUE possible
initial upper coordinates:

    r_0=ell*u+kappa*r-omega*w,
    w_0=(omega*r+kappa*w)/a.                        (17)

Consequently the original planar squared radius is exactly

    N0=(ell*u+kappa*r-omega*w)^2
                          +(omega*r+kappa*w)^2/a^2. (18)

This initial denominator is never replaced by r^2+w^2 at the switch.

For original time i>=1, let V_i=sum_(1<=k<i)h_k and let
b_i=product_(1<=k<i)(1-h_k), with V_1=0,b_1=1. Then the proposed lower
coordinates are

    r_i=r-u*V_i, w_i=b_i*w.                         (19)

These are actual iff every corresponding lower cell guard holds, along
with the initial upper cell in(17). We now eliminate the entire prefix
normal guard list analytically.

## 9. All original guards, chronological payment, and uniform strict bound

### 9.1 Exact guard reduction for this pattern

Tangential lower coordinates decrease monotonically with original mass.
Therefore every tangential guard in samples1,...,n is equivalent to

    u*(M+1)=u*(S-a)<=r<=u+Rside.                    (20)

By the refined prefix product in Section4, |b_i|<=beta for every i>=2.
The first of these is b_2=-beta, since the second physical entry of H_m
is1+d_1=1+beta. At i=1, b_1=1. Consequently ALL the lower normal guards
are equivalent to

    -v<=w<=v/beta.                                 (21)

Necessity uses i=1 and i=2. For sufficiency, if w>=0, negative b_i have
magnitude at most beta and positive ones cause no violation; if w<=0,
positive b_i have magnitude at most1, and negative ones cause no violation.
This includes the terminal, whose signed suffix factor is p_m/a>0.

The remaining exact guards are simply the initial upper ones:

    u<=ell*u+kappa*r-omega*w<=u+Rside,
    (omega*r+kappa*w)/a>=-v.                        (22)

Equations(20)-(22) are necessary AND sufficient: if they hold, (17)
projects to the upper side, its first update lands exactly at(r,w) in the
lower frame, and induction with(19) and the proved guard reduction keeps
every subsequent sample in the lower cell of the SAME finite triangle.
No remote normal or initial cost is being relaxed.

### 9.2 Geometry enforces a lower bound on the angle cosine

Every legal pattern satisfies u^2>1/4. To prove it, the left inequality in
(22) gives

    kappa*r>=omega*w-a*u>=-(2v^2+a)*u.

If kappa<=-1/2, it follows that
r<=2*(2v^2+a)*u<5u. But(20) and S>63 imply
r>=u*(S-a)>5u, a contradiction. Thus kappa>-1/2 and u^2=(1+kappa)/2>1/4.
Put k=u*r. It then follows that

    k> (S-a)/4>15.                                 (23)

This rules out a vanishing tangential height direction in THIS long
single-switch pattern by paying the full remaining physical mass. It is
not an angle restriction imposed on the original triangle family.

### 9.3 The original radius pays the rotated height and normal

Define the exact excess over the unrestarted comparison radius by

    Delta=N0-(r+ell*u)^2-w^2.

Since the rotation preserves r^2+w^2, expansion of(18) gives

    Delta=(a^(-2)-1)*(omega*r+kappa*w)^2
                    -2ell*u*((1-kappa)*r+omega*w). (24)

We prove Delta>0 on the WHOLE guarded domain. By(21), |w|<=v/beta.
Since |kappa|<1, omega=2u*v, beta>99/100 and(23),

    omega*r+kappa*w >= v*(2k-1/beta)>v*k>0.

Also1-kappa=2v^2, and hence

    2ell*u*((1-kappa)*r+omega*w)
       <=4ell*v^2*(k+u^2/beta)
       <6v^2*(k+2).

Using a<1/2 in(24) therefore gives

    Delta>v^2*(3k^2-6k-12)>0.                      (25)

For the last strict inequality, k>15>4 and the polynomial is positive
at4 and increasing for k>=4. All these are exact symbolic comparisons.
In particular this coupling is UNIFORM in all legal u,v,r,w,Rside; it
has no hidden geometry-dependent threshold or constant.

### 9.4 Full terminal objective and evaluated payment identity

The entire original suffix, of even length n-1, has signed normal factor
p_m/a. Equations(4),(19) give the ACTUAL terminal objective

    E_num=2phi(z_n)=2u*r-Drem*u^2+(Q/a^2)*w^2.       (26)

Combine(24) with one square expansion:

    N0-D*E_num
       =Delta+[r-(D-ell)*u]^2+[1-D*Q/a^2]*w^2>0.   (27)

The square identity uses Drem=D-2ell exactly. Its normal coefficient is
strictly positive by Section4, and Delta>0 by(25). Thus

    E_plane=E_num/N0<1/D                            (28)

for EVERY legal member of this pattern at EVERY enclosing m>=1536.
Since Q<1/D, the exact finite-tilt formula(16) also gives E_T<1/D for
every finite T>0. No all-start triangle statement is inferred from this.

This is the chronological counterpart of the segment exterior pairing for
this one permitted switch: the first leaf's inverse amplifies the original
normal by1/a, the rotation mixes it into the future height direction, and
(24)-(27) pay both with the original initial radius. Omitting that inverse
cost would erase Delta and could produce an incorrect gain. The proof does
not use scalar sign ordering or assume a positive-to-positive persistence
property in the plane.

## 10. An explicit finite legal full-word switch family

The domain just bounded is nonempty at every m>=1536. Fix once and for all

    u=sqrt(3)/2, v=1/2, kappa=1/2, omega=sqrt(3)/2,
    r=(D-ell)*u, w=0,
    r_0=ell*u+kappa*r, w_0=omega*r/a,
    Rside=r+r_0+1, L=u*Rside, H=v*Rside,
    z_0=r_0*e_++w_0*n_+.

These are finite explicit parameters, with no scan or parameter optimization.
The initial upper tangential projection is r_0-u=a*u+kappa*r>0 and is
strictly less than Rside. Its normal is w_0+v>0. The first leaf gives
z_1=r*e_- exactly by(17). For every later original sample,

    z_i=[r-u*V_i]*e_-, 1<=i<=n.

The smallest tangential coordinate is at the terminal:

    r-u*M=u*(S+1)>u.

The largest is r<u+Rside. Every normal coordinate is0, so w_i+v=v>0.
Thus every original queried projection and the terminal projection lies
strictly inside its stated side; the first leaf is a genuine face switch.
The start is off-axis since t_0=v*r_0+u*w_0>0. The next point has negative
second coordinate -v*r, so this is not an axis trajectory or a relabeling
of one unchanged side. Its two side tangent spaces are distinct.

The full planar terminal numerator and original squared radius are

    2phi(z_n)=D*u^2,
    N0=u^2*[(ell+kappa*(D-ell))^2
                              +(omega^2/a^2)*(D-ell)^2].

Hence its exact planar score is

    E_plane=D/[(ell+kappa*(D-ell))^2
                              +(omega^2/a^2)*(D-ell)^2]<1/D. (29)

For a fully concrete orthogonal instance choose T=1 and
X_0=(s_0,t_0,-s_0), with
s_0=u*r_0-v*w_0 and t_0=v*r_0+u*w_0. Equation(15), with this ACTUAL s_0,
is its finite three-dimensional score and true denominator. Equation(28)
proves the same strict bound. No infinite tilt is used to assert a strict
witness. Fixed-angle rotation creates a positive initial-distance cost;
there is no gain toward17/(16D) in this whole pattern.

## 11. Literature, shared-state recall and remaining interface

The original problem, TASK.md, latest guidance5c716c2407472849, accepted
factsd8b59c7d92425121 and931b9653ce0f7201, and the worker-local previous
report5960de36af8e17d5 were read. The attempted project-root path for the
previous report returned `No such file or directory`; its actual own-worker
copy was then read. Shared recall included the centered-triangle interface
7662d3077f76bf26 /4bbd76cf65b3ec5a and earlier geometry dead ends. None is
used as a mathematical premise here. The centered support-envelope triangle
in that sibling report is a different objective from the present affine-
height Moreau envelope.

Search stage: active_program. Program: one fixed exposed affine-height
triangle under the SAME-m word. Missing mechanism: a coupled height/normal
invariant that survives actual face changes with the original radius.
Search mode: repair; intent: theorem/background. Matlas returned constrained
shadow-walk/Frank-Wolfe/projection lemmas and later stochastic/mirror-descent
results, none applicable to this fixed long-step GD word. Web search located
the silver-step literature. The exact existing local text
`downloads/release_middle_sector/2309.16530.txt` was read at its definitions,
normalization and recursive-gluing proof: Jason M. Altschuler and Pablo A.
Parrilo, "Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for
Smooth Convex Optimization", arXiv:2309.16530 (2023),
https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530 . Its silver schedule is not the perturbed
same-m word here. The proof uses long-range consistency, not a one-step
radius envelope, and supplies no certificate for the present word. No
external theorem or numerical check is used in this report.

The first direct attempt was scalar distance/capture migration; Sections6.1
and6.2 are exact legal failures. The repaired direct attempt retains the
side height slope and normal through complete children, giving(12), but
it has an unproved interface at arbitrary face changes. Mechanism2 closes
one such interface only for the first-leaf switch with the full suffix on
the opposite side, by(20)-(27). Its legal all-depth example is Section10.
This is the end of the two-mechanism allocation, not permission to open a
third pattern or another geometric family.

The remaining problem is genuinely quantitative: for arbitrary original
face histories, either control the sum of rotation/cap costs by one global
original-radius inequality at scale(1+C/m)/D with absolute C, or construct
one fully guarded all-depth history whose paid finite-tilt score exceeds
17/(16D). A face-cell enumeration, a one-step tube, a terminal small-radius
claim, or a fixed-side bound does not establish that interface. The
present report leaves it OPEN and does not regard it as routine.

The unchanged original target is the complete all-horizon predetermined-
nonnegative-step minimax profile in PROBLEM.md. No claimed result here
replaces that target, changes W=2R, or fulfills the full-class hypothesis
of931b9653ce0f7201. Return at cap1; no automatic renewal.

# Common-prefix characters: strict local slack and a remote-pair obstruction

Status: **self-contained, unverified joint candidate from the user-directed
continuation after the prior seventeen-round launch**. No solver, parameter
sweep, installation, fact submission, new launch, or finalization is used.
The sole correctness predecessor is accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for
the necessity and orientation of the original interpolation inequalities.
Earlier common-prefix findings motivate the construction; they are not proof
premises. All identities needed below are derived here.

This specifies one new dependent, horizon-dependent probability law and one
positive amplitude law with strict local backoff. The latter is impossible:
four original remote interpolation inequalities cannot all hold. It does
**not** exclude every amplitude law for this probability distribution, and
does not establish either resolution of the original minimax problem.

## 1. Complete finite policy and the claim

Fix an integer m>=65536. Set

    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m, c_chr=sigma(1+1/m),
    a_chr=delta/c_chr, N=2^m, n=N-1,
    epsilon=1/N, eta=1/N^2,
    l=1+a_chr, p_chr=1-epsilon, alpha=1-2epsilon,
    beta=7/10, D_chr=1+l p_chr,
    u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1), k>=1.

The actual algorithm uses

    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_chr c_chr^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Equivalently, its original step i is
h_i=1+a_chr c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n. A leaf has nu_2(i+1)=0
and step l; a level-k bridge, k>=1, has step 1+u_k. Every subword below
retains the same enclosing-m parameters. No chronological index, step,
physical horizon, or benchmark is changed.

Give sample i its standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For a
character index s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) in {0,1}^m define

    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),
    B(s)=(-1)^s_0,
    C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m.

The map s -> (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective: (-1)^s_1=C_1 and
(-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Thus the following specifies EVERY atom.
Write mu_1=-7/10 and mu_(-1)=9/10, and for b in {-1,1} put

    A_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))
       =[(1+mu_b)/2] 1_(all c_j=1)
          +[(1-mu_b)/2] 1_(all c_j=-1),
    P_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))=product_(j=1)^(m-1)(1+mu_b c_j)/2,

    w_s=[(1+p_chr B(s))/2]
          [(1-eta) A_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))
              +eta P_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))].       (1)

Here 1_(condition) equals one or zero according to the displayed condition.
Each conditional factor is a probability law. Their mixture sums to one;
every w_s is strictly positive since epsilon,eta>0 and |mu_b|<1.
The first component makes ALL prefix signs equal, including the two lowest
ones. The second component gives full support at a stated rate.

Define the positive odd-to-odd amplitude ratios

    q_1=47/100, q_2=99/100, q_3=21/10,
    q_4=9/2, q_5=99/10,
    q_k=(69/100)u_k/D_chr, k>=6.                          (2)

The amplitudes of EVERY original sample are fixed before the trajectory:

    Gamma_0=1,
    Gamma_(i+1)=alpha Gamma_i                  at a leaf,
    Gamma_(i+1)=(q_k/alpha) Gamma_i             at a level-k bridge.
                                                                    (3)

Prescribe the actual real vectors in R^N

    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s, 0<=i<=n.            (4)

For any common anchor X_0 and ANY real values f_0,...,f_n, define the original
positions and residuals

    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.            (5)

The claim has two parts.

* Every adjacent cocoercivity sum Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i) is strictly negative.
  Every sum between the two odd endpoints of a bridge and its following
  leaf is also strictly negative. These sums do not depend on f or X_0.
* Let K=2^10=1024. At least one of

      Q_(0,K), Q_(K,0), Q_(3,K+3), Q_(K+3,3)              (6)

  is greater than 1/50, for EVERY f and EVERY X_0.

Thus (1)--(4) cannot be the gradient transcript of one convex differentiable
1-smooth function, with or without a minimizer. The first assertion is only
about necessary pair sums; it does not assert interpolation from local tests.
The second assertion retains four actual ordered inequalities and is a
uniform, finite-horizon obstruction to this entire specified policy.

## 2. Exact kernel moments and bounds

For a binary difference d=i xor j, put d_m=0. Inserting the inverse relations
between s and its prefix signs gives

    chi_s(d)=B(s)^d_0 product_(j=1)^(m-1) C_j(s)^(d_j xor d_(j+1)),
    <g_i,g_j>/(Gamma_i Gamma_j)=E chi_s(i xor j).            (7)

Expectations here are finite sums against (1). Conditional on B=b, every
C_j has mean mu_b in BOTH components of (1). Consequently

    E B=p_chr=1-epsilon,
    E C_k=z_chr=-7/10+4epsilon/5,
    E[B C_k]=w_chr=-7/10-epsilon/10.                       (8)

At a bridge of level k and its following leaf, the relative character
signs at the three ORIGINAL samples are 1,B C_k,C_k. Their correlations
are w_chr,p_chr,z_chr, respectively. At a leaf, the adjacent correlation is
p_chr. Multiplication by any starting character cancels in all these
inner products, so these identities hold at every occurrence.

This law lies outside the previous low-joint-moment chart:

    E C_1 C_2=1-eta+eta(49/100+4epsilon/25)>1/2.            (9)

To check (9), the common component has product one, and the independent
component has conditional product mu_B^2. Averaging over B gives
49/100+4epsilon/25. The final inequality uses eta<=10^(-6).

We use only the following rational bounds:

    0<epsilon<=10^(-6), 9999/10000<delta<1,
    12/5<c_chr<2417/1000,
    1413/1000<l<283/200,
    12/5<D_chr<483/200,
    999/1000<alpha<1, alpha>=1-2*10^(-6).                  (10)

For completeness 707/500<sqrt(2)<99/70 follows by squaring positive
rationals. Hence c_chr>1207/500>12/5 and, using m>=1024,
c_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<2417/1000. Also
a_chr>(70/169)(1023/1025)>413/1000 and
a_chr<sqrt(2)-1<29/70<83/200, giving the bounds on l.
Then D_chr>1+(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>12/5 and D_chr<483/200.
The stronger lower bound on delta follows from m>=65536>10000.
The bounds on epsilon and alpha follow directly from their definitions.

Define D_tilde=1+l p_chr/alpha and C_tilde=-z_chr-l w_chr/alpha.
The same inequalities give

    D_tilde<4831/2000,
    C_tilde<1691/1000,
    z_chr<-699999/1000000,
    D_tilde/D_chr<=1/alpha<1001/1000,
    1/alpha<100001/100000.                                (11)

For the first two, use l/alpha<(283/200)/(1-2*10^(-6)),
-z_chr<=7/10, and -w_chr<=7/10+10^(-7). The ratio bound follows from
D_tilde=D_chr/alpha+1-1/alpha<=D_chr/alpha. Each strict numerical comparison
in (11) is a rational inequality with positive denominators.

## 3. Strict local slack without saturation

Accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` states that an actual smooth-convex
transcript must obey every Q_ij<=0, with exactly the sign convention (5).
For adjacent samples with step 1+u, unit-direction correlation d, and
positive norm ratio r, adding its two original inequalities gives

    [Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i)]/Gamma_i^2
          =r^2+(u-1)d r-u.                                (12)

At a leaf u=a_chr, d=p_chr, r=alpha. Substitution yields

    alpha^2+(a_chr-1)p_chr alpha-a_chr
       =-(1+3a_chr)epsilon+(2+2a_chr)epsilon^2<0.           (13)

Now let u=u_k and q=q_k. The bridge has next norm ratio q/alpha;
the following leaf produces norm ratio q from the original odd starting
sample. Directly expanding the pair sum between those odd endpoints gives

    [Q_(before,after)+Q_(after,before)]/Gamma_before^2
       =D_tilde q^2+(z_chr u+C_tilde)q-u.                  (14)

The intervening gradient has norm (q/alpha)Gamma_before and is included:
X_after-X_before=-(1+u)g_before-l g_middle. Substituting (8) into this
exact displacement gives (14), not an identification or deletion of the
middle vector.

For k=1,...,5 define the explicit lower bound

    u_low(k)=(9999/10000)(12/5)^(k-1).

By (10),(11), (14) is bounded above by

    (4831/2000)q_k^2
       +[1691/1000-(699999/1000000)u_low(k)]q_k-u_low(k).   (15)

All five rational expressions (15) are negative. Their negativity can be
checked by inserting the five fractions (2), multiplying by the positive
denominators, and comparing integers. The accompanying fixed audit records
these five comparisons; no optimization or parameter search is involved.

For the bridge alone at k=1, put r_hi=(100001/100000)q_1. Since u_1<1,
(12) is at most

    r_hi^2+(700001/1000000)(1-u_low(1))r_hi-u_low(1)<0.     (16)

For k=2,...,5, where u_k>1, replace only the positive square upwards and
the negative product downwards. The resulting upper bound is

    [(100001/100000)q_k]^2
           -(7/10)(u_low(k)-1)q_k-u_low(k)<0.             (17)

Again these are four fixed rational comparisons, supplied by the fractions
in (2). There is no saturation at any of these levels.

For k>=6, write gamma=69/100, so q=gamma u/D_chr. In (14), (11) gives

    D_tilde q^2+z_chr u q
       <=[(1001/1000)(69/100)-699/1000]u q<0,
    C_tilde q-u<[(1691/1000)(69/100)/(12/5)-1]u<0.         (18)

This proves strictness for every such odd endpoint comparison, including
arbitrarily high bridges of the ORIGINAL horizon. For the bridge itself,
r=q/alpha<(3/10)u and u>=u_low(6)>2. Since w_chr<=-7/10,

    r^2+(u-1)w_chr r-u
       <-(4/10)u r+(7/10)r-u<0.                          (19)

Equations (13)--(19) prove all stated local slacks. They do not rely on a
maximal adjacent root, nor on either low joint moment being at most 1/2.

## 4. An expanded copy inside the original fixed prefix

Let J_0=() and J_j=(J_(j-1),j,J_(j-1)) be a list of bridge levels. This
only indexes odd-to-odd transitions, with both actual steps retained in
(3) and (14). Set

    T_0=1, T_j=q_j T_(j-1)^2.                             (20)

Counting the transitions before sample 2^j-1 gives
Gamma_(2^j-1)=alpha T_(j-1). The next bridge has level j, so

    Gamma_(2^j)=q_j T_(j-1).                              (21)

For j=10 write r=Gamma_K with K=1024. We show r>2 using small rational
lower bounds alone. Formula (2) and (10) give

    q_6>(69/100)(9999/10000)(12/5)^5/(483/200)>4549/200.

Since q_(k+1)/q_k=c_chr>12/5 for k>=6, valid lower bounds for q_6,...,q_10
are, in order,

    227/10, 109/2, 131, 314, 754.                         (22)

Starting with T_0=1, lower bounds for T_1,...,T_9 are

    47/100, 21869/100000, 10043/100000,
    4538/100000, 2038/100000, 942/100000,
    483/100000, 305/100000, 292/100000.                    (23)

For each entry its predecessor squared, multiplied by the corresponding
q in (2) or the lower bound (22), is at least that entry. These nine
inequalities are integer comparisons after clearing denominators. Thus

    r=q_10 T_9>754*(292/100000)>2.                         (24)

The first three steps after K have levels 0,1,0, exactly as at the start.
Consequently the first and repeated four-sample amplitude patterns are

    (Gamma_0,Gamma_1,Gamma_2,Gamma_3)=(1,alpha,q_1,alpha q_1),
    (Gamma_K,Gamma_(K+1),Gamma_(K+2),Gamma_(K+3))
                        =r(1,alpha,q_1,alpha q_1).        (25)

All these are actual indices well before n. No terminal padding, restart,
rescaling of the second subproblem's physical parameters, or new algorithm
is involved.

## 5. Matched original pairs in the exact reference Gram

Introduce an AUXILIARY reference probability: B=1 and C_1=...=C_(m-1)=V,
where P(V=1)=3/20 and P(V=-1)=17/20. Its direction vectors repeat

    e_+, e_+, e_-, e_-, ...,
    e_+=(sqrt(3/20),sqrt(17/20)),
    e_-=(sqrt(3/20),-sqrt(17/20)),
    <e_+,e_->=-beta.                                     (26)

Indeed only character bit s_1 can be nonzero under this measure.
Use exactly the SAME amplitudes (3), steps, values, and sample indices
as in the finite policy. Only the direction Gram is changed in this
auxiliary calculation. It is not assumed legal or obtained by a limiting
argument.

Let Z=X_K-X_0 in this reference transcript. Positive amplitudes and steps
give

    Z=-P_+ e_+-P_- e_-, P_+,P_->=0.                       (27)

Put q=q_1, g=alpha q, and

    A_len=l+(1+delta)alpha, B_len=l q,
    d_3=A_len e_++B_len e_-.

By the first three actual updates, X_3=X_0-d_3. By (25),
X_(K+3)=X_K-r d_3, and the two matched gradient pairs are
(e_+,r e_+) and (g e_-,r g e_-).

Write R_i^0=Q_(i,K+i)^0+Q_(K+i,i)^0 for i=0,3, where the superscript
marks only this reference Gram. Adding the two original ordered residuals
cancels all values and gives

    R_0^0=(r-1)[(r-1)-<e_+,Z>],
    R_3^0=(r-1)g[(r-1)(g+<e_-,d_3>)-<e_-,Z>].            (28)

With the POSITIVE weight beta/g, their sum is EXACTLY

    S^0=R_0^0+(beta/g)R_3^0
       =(r-1)[(r-1)kappa+(1-beta^2)P_+],                 (29)
    kappa=1+beta q(alpha+l)-beta^2[l+(1+delta)alpha].

To check the final term, <e_++beta e_-,e_->=0 and
<e_++beta e_-,e_+>=1-beta^2. Substituting (27) therefore gives
-<e_++beta e_-,Z>=(1-beta^2)P_+>=0. This is the specific geometric
information provided by the common-prefix law.

Using (10), q=47/100, and (1+delta)alpha<2,

    kappa>1+(7/10)(47/100)(999/1000+1413/1000)
               -(49/100)(283/200+2)
         =60099/500000>3/25.                             (30)

Equations (24),(27),(29) imply S^0>3/25. This lower bound depends on
neither anchor nor values. It is a sum of four original ordered residuals,
not a chosen objective, a norm to the origin, or a surrogate distance.

The same exact identity yields a useful narrower interface: in the
two-ray reference, ANY repeated pattern (1,alpha,q,alpha q) with kappa>0
cannot expand by a factor r>1 after positive steps, regardless of all the
other amplitudes. With the parameter bounds (10), every q>=2/5 has

    kappa>1+(7/10)(2/5)(999/1000+1413/1000)
                  -(49/100)(283/200+2)=201/100000>0.       (31)

The finite full-support statement proved next concerns the definite law
(2),(3); (31) alone is not a general perturbation theorem for arbitrary
amplitudes or arbitrarily late repeated blocks.

## 6. Finite full support: explicit perturbation bound

The weight of the B=1 common component in (1) is
(1-epsilon/2)(1-eta). Conditional on this component the measure is exactly
(26). Hence for ALL binary labels i,j, the normalized kernels satisfy

    |K_ij-K_ij^0|<=epsilon+2eta=:d_err<=2epsilon.           (32)

This follows by writing the probability as its displayed reference
component plus the remaining probability. Every character product is in
[-1,1], so the difference of expectations is at most twice the remaining
mass. It is a direct finite probability bound, including high binary
labels; no condition number or inverse Gram is used.

Here are deliberately conservative bounds on the fixed prefix through K+3.
The largest bridge level there is ten. Since c_chr<5/2,

    u_k<(5/2)^9<2^12, h_i<2^13,
    q_k<2^12, q_k/alpha<2^13                       (k<=10).

There are fewer than K+4 updates before any of those samples. Thus, putting

    U=2^(14(K+4)),

we have Gamma_i<U there, and

    sum_(t<K+3) h_t Gamma_t<(K+4)2^13 U<2^24 U.            (33)

These loose bounds are not computational estimates; they follow by
multiplying the scalar ratio bounds. Their only purpose is to make the
finite perturbation cost explicit.

For any two samples i<j in this prefix, their pair sum has the original
formula

    R_ij=||g_i-g_j||^2
             -sum_(t=i)^(j-1) h_t <g_i-g_j,g_t>.          (34)

The diagonal norm terms are unchanged because both probability measures
sum to one. Equations (32),(33) give

    |R_ij-R_ij^0|
       <=2d_err Gamma_i Gamma_j
           +d_err(Gamma_i+Gamma_j)sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t Gamma_t
       <2^26 d_err U^2.                                 (35)

Use the SAME positive coefficient beta/g on the finite-policy pair sum,

    S=R_(0,K)+(beta/g)R_(3,K+3).

Since alpha>999/1000 and q=47/100, beta/g<3/2. In particular its total
weight is less than four, so (35) yields

    |S-S^0|<2^28 d_err U^2
              <=2^(29-m+28(K+4))=2^(28813-m)<2^(-100).    (36)

The final inequality holds for every original m>=65536. Although very
conservative, it is uniform in the original horizon. Combining (30),(36),

    S>3/25-2^(-100)>11/100.                              (37)

If all four ordered residuals in (6) were at most 1/50, their positive
weighted sum would be at most

    [2+2beta/g]/50<5/50=1/10,

contradicting (37). At least one is therefore greater than 1/50, as claimed.
Every function value and every common anchor was arbitrary. Positive defects
have not been hidden in a vanishing perturbation or discarded as negligible.

One can also set epsilon=eta=0 in the definitions and take alpha=1. The
reference and proposed Gram then coincide; (20)--(30) give the obstruction
directly at that boundary, while the leaf pair sums equal zero. The positive
finite laws are already excluded by (32)--(37), independently of any
continuity claim at the singular measure.

## 7. Scope, checks, and the remaining policy problem

The explicit law has positive weights at every finite horizon, depends on
the ORIGINAL horizon through epsilon and eta, couples the two lowest
prefix signs, and uses nonsaturating positive amplitudes. It changes the
hypotheses that supported the previous moment obstruction. Nevertheless,
the two retained remote pairs detect an impossible repeated expansion.
Every unused original normal and interpolation inequality remains a
requirement; four of them already suffice to rule out this candidate.

This is an infeasibility result for (1)--(4), so it asserts no payoff for an
illegal trajectory. There is no closest-minimizer distance to replace by
the anchor norm. The contradiction holds before selecting any function,
anchor, or polar minimizer, and consequently also covers an anchored
projection construction with a nonzero closest polar point. C62 is not
used. Accepted finite C59 and unrestricted character constructions are
outside the asserted scope.

In particular, the probability measure (1) is STILL OPEN for other
amplitude laws. The present rule has a uniform ratio on all occurrences
of a given physical level; that creates the matched copy (25). Avoiding
this obstruction requires a different history-dependent amplitude rule,
enough backoff to avoid the expanded copy, or a change of the specified
measure/labels. Formula (31) shows a real cost of repeated-shape expansion
in the singular two-ray geometry; it is not an all-policy payoff bound.

The fixed audit `check_common_prefix_remote_pairs.py` checks five local
odd-endpoint and bridge estimates, the nine rational growth comparisons,
one exact matched-pair identity, a 32-atom finite mixture fixture, and the
displayed exponent arithmetic. All checks passed in about 0.002 seconds.
They are transcription checks on the proof's fractions, not an optimizer,
sampled maximum, or a replacement for verification.

Matlas returned a self-contracted-gradient theorem with steps at most 1/L
or backtracking, hypotheses violated by H_m, and other inapplicable
constant-step results. Web background returned smooth convex interpolation;
its needed necessity is already proved in the read accepted predecessor
`1252b8823bb5a290`. No external theorem is a mathematical premise here.

The entire package remains **UNVERIFIED**. No fact submission is authorized
by the current task. The original all-schedule minimax question and a fixed
asymptotic algorithmic improvement remain unresolved.
